(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
, we define the normalized weights

\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)=\frac{1}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}{f^{\prime\prime}_{\theta}(a|s)}\kern 5.0pt.

(42)

The following lemma provides bounds on several key quantities that will appear in the appendix.

Lemma B.6
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
and
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
, respectively. For any parameter
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\omega_{f}\kern 5.0pt,\quad\left\|\operatorname{Y}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\mathrm{y}_{f}\kern 5.0pt,\quad\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\operatorname{D}^{f}(\pi_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|s)\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))\leq\mathrm{d}_{f}\kern 5.0pt,\quad\max_{(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}}\frac{\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}\leq\omega_{f}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

The proof immediately follows from the definition of the different quantities and
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
.
∎

Using
Lemma

B.4
, we get the following expression for the derivatives of the policy.

Corollary B.7
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
and
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
, respectively. Then the policy
\pi_{\theta}^{f}
is twice continuously differentiable with respect to
\theta
.
Additionally, for all
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
and
s\in\mathcal{S}
, there exists a unique
\mu_{\theta}(s)\in\mathbb{R}
such that for any
a\in\mathcal{A}
, we have

\displaystyle\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)=\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)[f^{\prime}]^{-1}\left(\theta(s,a)-\mu_{\theta}(s))\right).

(43)

For any
s\in\mathcal{S}
, the
\theta\mapsto\mu_{\theta}(s)
is continuously differentiable. For any
s^{\prime}\neq s
,
\partial/\partial\theta(s^{\prime},\cdot)\mu_{\theta}(s)=0
and

\frac{\partial\mu_{\theta}(s)}{\partial\theta(s,\cdot)}=\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|s)\kern 5.0pt.

Moreover, for any
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
,
s\in\mathcal{S}
, and
(a,b)\in\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{A}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\cdot\frac{\partial\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}=\mathsf{1}_{b}(a)\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)-\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(b|s)\kern 5.0pt,

In addition, for any
(a,b,c)\in\mathcal{A}^{3}
, the second derivative satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\cdot\frac{\partial\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial\theta(s,b)\partial\theta(s,c)}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{1}_{b}(a)\mathsf{1}_{c}(a)\frac{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(a|s\right)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(a|s)^{2}}\cdot\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)+\mathsf{1}_{c}(b)\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(b|s)\frac{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(b|s\right)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(b|s\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathsf{1}_{b}(a)\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(c|s)\frac{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(a|s\right)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(a|s\right)^{2}}+\mathsf{1}_{c}(a)\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(b|s)\frac{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(a|s\right)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(a|s\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)\cdot\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(b|s)\cdot\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(c|s)\cdot\left[\frac{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}(a|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(a|s)^{2}}+\frac{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}(b|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(b|s)^{2}}+\frac{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}(c|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(c|s)^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)\cdot\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(b|s)\cdot\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(c|s)\cdot\sum_{d\in\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(d|s)\cdot\frac{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(d|s\right)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(d|s\right)^{2}}\kern 5.0pt.

Lemma B.8
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
and
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
, respectively. Then, it holds that

\displaystyle\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{A}^{2}}\left|\frac{\partial\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\right|\leq 2\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\kern 5.0pt,\quad\sum_{(a,b,c)\in\mathcal{A}^{3}}\left|\frac{\partial^{2}\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial\theta(s,b)\partial\theta(s,c)}\right|\leq 8\kappa_{f}\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

Using the expression of the derivative of the policy provided in
Corollary

B.7
, we have by the triangle inequality

\displaystyle\sum_{b\in\mathcal{A}}\left|\frac{\partial\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\right|

\displaystyle=\sum_{b\in\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)\left|\mathsf{1}_{b}(a)-\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(b|s)\right|=2\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)(1-\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s))\kern 5.0pt.

where we used that

\displaystyle\sum_{b\in\mathcal{A}}\left|\mathsf{1}_{b}(a)-\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(b|s)\right|=2(1-\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s))\kern 5.0pt.

(44)

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{A}^{2}}\left|\frac{\partial\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\right|\leq 2\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\kern 5.0pt.

Fix
a\in\mathcal{A}
. By using the expression of the second derivative of the policy provided in
Corollary

B.7
combined with the triangle inequality and (
44
), we get

\displaystyle\sum_{(b,c)\in\mathcal{A}^{2}}\left|\frac{\partial^{2}\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial\theta(s,b)\partial\theta(s,c)}\right|\leq 4\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\frac{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}(a|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(a|s)^{2}}\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)+4\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)\sum_{b\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}(b|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(b|s)^{2}}\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(b|s)\kern 5.0pt.

Next combining
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and (
44
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{(b,c)\in\mathcal{A}^{2}}\left|\frac{\partial^{2}\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial\theta(s,b)\partial\theta(s,c)}\right|\leq 8\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)\kappa_{f}\kern 5.0pt.

Finally, summing over the actions concludes the proof.
∎

B.4
Smoothness of Objective

Firstly, we recall that the regularized value satisfies the following fixed-point equation
[
9
]
:

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}^{f}(s)

\displaystyle=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\pi(a|s)\mathsf{r}(s,a)-\lambda\,\operatorname{D}^{f}(\pi(\cdot|s)\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))+\gamma\sum_{(a,s^{\prime})\in\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{S}}\pi(a|s)\mathsf{P}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\,v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}^{f}(s^{\prime})\kern 5.0pt.

(45)

In order to prove the smoothness of the objective, we will prove that the all the second-order directional derivatives are bounded.
Denote
\theta_{\alpha}=\theta+\alpha u
where
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
and
u\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
. By
Equation

45
, for any
s\in\mathcal{S}
it holds that

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}(s)=\mathbf{e}_{s}^{\top}M(\alpha)\mathsf{r}_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}\kern 5.0pt,

(46)

where
M(\alpha)
is a matrix of
\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{S}}
defined by

\displaystyle M(\alpha)=\left(\mathrm{Id}-\gamma\mathsf{P}_{\theta_{\alpha}}\right)^{-1}\kern 5.0pt,

and where
\mathsf{r}_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}
, and
\mathsf{P}_{\theta_{\alpha}}
are defined in
Section

2
.
Taking the derivative of (
46
) with respect to
\alpha
yields

\displaystyle\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}(s)}{\partial\alpha}=\gamma\mathbf{e}_{s}^{\top}M(\alpha)\frac{\partial\mathsf{P}_{\theta_{\alpha}}}{\partial\alpha}M(\alpha)\mathsf{r}_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}+\mathbf{e}_{s}^{\top}M(\alpha)\frac{\partial\mathsf{r}_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}}{\partial\alpha}\kern 5.0pt.

Taking the derivative of the preceding equation with respect to
\alpha
gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}(s)}{\partial\alpha^{2}}

\displaystyle=2\gamma^{2}\mathbf{e}_{s}^{\top}M(\alpha)\frac{\partial\mathsf{P}_{\theta_{\alpha}}}{\partial\alpha}M(\alpha)\frac{\partial\mathsf{P}_{\theta_{\alpha}}}{\partial\alpha}M(\alpha)\mathsf{r}_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}+\gamma\mathbf{e}_{s}^{\top}M(\alpha)\frac{\partial^{2}\mathsf{P}_{\theta_{\alpha}}}{\partial^{2}\alpha}M(\alpha)\mathsf{r}_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}

\displaystyle+2\gamma\mathbf{e}_{s}^{\top}M(\alpha)\frac{\partial\mathsf{P}_{\theta_{\alpha}}}{\partial\alpha}M(\alpha)\frac{\partial\mathsf{r}_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}}{\partial\alpha}+\mathbf{e}_{s}^{\top}M(\alpha)\frac{\partial^{2}\mathsf{r}_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}}{\partial^{2}\alpha}\kern 5.0pt.

(47)

In order to control the second-order directional derivative of the regularised value function, we establish first several properties of the quantities that appear in the preceding equality.

Lemma B.9
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
and
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
, respectively. We have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial\mathsf{P}_{\theta_{\alpha}}}{\partial\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right\|_{\infty}\leq 2\max_{s}\{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\}\left\|u\right\|_{2}\kern 5.0pt,

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial^{2}\mathsf{P}_{\theta_{\alpha}}}{\partial^{2}\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right\|_{\infty}\leq 8\kappa_{f}\max_{s}\{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\}\left\|u\right\|_{2}^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

Bounding the first-order directional derivative.

The derivative with respect to
\alpha
is

\displaystyle\left[\frac{\partial\mathsf{P}_{\theta_{\alpha}}}{\partial\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right]_{s,s^{\prime}}=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\left[\frac{\partial\pi_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right]\mathsf{P}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\kern 5.0pt.

Fix
s\in\mathcal{S}
. Because
\pi_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}(a|s)
depends only on
\theta(s,\cdot)
, by the chain rule

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\left|\frac{\partial\pi_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right|=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\left|\langle\frac{\partial\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial\theta(s,\cdot)},u(s,\cdot)\rangle\right|\leq\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\left\|\frac{\partial\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial\theta(s,\cdot)}\right\|_{1}\left\|u(s,\cdot)\right\|_{2}\kern 5.0pt,

where in the last inequality we used Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the fact that the
L_{1}
norm dominates the
L_{2}
norm. Now using
Lemma

B.8
, we get that

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\left\|\frac{\partial\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial\theta(s,\cdot)}\right\|_{1}\left\|u(s,\cdot)\right\|_{2}\leq 2\left\|u\right\|_{2}\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\kern 5.0pt.

Bounding the second-order directional derivative.

Similarly, taking the second derivative with respect to
\alpha
yields

\displaystyle\left[\frac{\partial^{2}\mathsf{P}_{\theta_{\alpha}}}{\partial^{2}\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right]_{s,s^{\prime}}=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}\pi_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial^{2}\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right]\mathsf{P}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\kern 5.0pt.

Fix
s\in\mathcal{S}
. It holds that

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\left|\frac{\partial^{2}\pi_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial^{2}\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right|=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\left|\langle\frac{\partial^{2}\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial^{2}\theta(s,\cdot)}u(s,\cdot),u(s,\cdot)\rangle\right|\leq\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\left\|\frac{\partial^{2}\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial^{2}\theta(s,\cdot)}\right\|_{2}\left\|u(s,\cdot)\right\|_{2}^{2}\kern 5.0pt,

where in the last inequality, we used the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the definition of the matrix operator norm. Additionally, using that for any matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|A\right\|_{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}|a_{i,j}|

combined with
Lemma

B.8
, we get that

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\left|\frac{\partial^{2}\pi_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial^{2}\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right|\leq 8\left\|u(s,\cdot)\right\|_{2}^{2}\kappa_{f}\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)

∎

Lemma B.10
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
and
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
, respectively. Then, the regularized reward satisfies

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{r}_{\theta}^{f}\|_{\infty}\leq 1+\lambda\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\operatorname{D}^{f}({\pi_{\theta}^{f}}(\cdot|s)\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))\kern 5.0pt.

Additionally, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial\mathsf{r}_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}}{\partial\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right\|_{\infty}\leq 2\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left\{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)+\lambda\operatorname{Y}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\right\}\left\|u\right\|_{2}\kern 5.0pt,

and that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial^{2}\mathsf{r}_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}}{\partial^{2}\alpha^{2}}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left\{4(2\kappa_{f}+\lambda)\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)+8\lambda\kappa_{f}\operatorname{Y}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\right\}\left\|u\right\|_{2}^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

The bound on
\|\mathsf{r}_{\theta}^{f}\|_{\infty}
is immediate.

Bounding the first derivative.
It holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial\mathsf{r}_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}}{\partial\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right\|_{\infty}=\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left|\langle\frac{\partial\mathsf{r}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}{\partial\theta},u\rangle\right|=\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left|\langle\frac{\partial\mathsf{r}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}{\partial\theta(s,\cdot)},u(s,\cdot)\rangle\right|\leq\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left\|\frac{\partial\mathsf{r}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}{\partial\theta(s,\cdot)}\right\|_{1}\left\|u\right\|_{\infty}\kern 5.0pt.

Computing the derivative of
\mathsf{r}_{\theta}^{f}(s)
with respect to
\theta(s,b)
yields

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathsf{r}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\partial\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\mathsf{r}(s,a)-\lambda\frac{\partial\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Plugging in the expression of the derivative of the policy of
Corollary

B.7
in the preceding identity yields

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\tilde{\mathsf{r}}_{\theta}(s)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(b|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(b|s)}\mathsf{r}(s,b)-\lambda\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(b|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(b|s)}f_{\theta}^{\prime}(b|s)-\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(b|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(a|s)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(b|s)}\cdot\frac{\mathsf{r}(s,a)}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}

\displaystyle+\lambda\frac{1}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(b|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(a|s)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(b|s)}f_{\theta}^{\prime}(a|s)\kern 5.0pt.

(48)

Taking the absolute value, applying the triangle inequality, and using that the rewards are bounded by
1
gives

\displaystyle\sum_{b\in\mathcal{A}}\left|\frac{\partial\mathsf{r}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\right|\leq 2\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)+2\lambda\operatorname{Y}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\kern 5.0pt.

Bounding the second derivative.

It holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial^{2}\mathsf{r}_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}}{\partial\alpha^{2}}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left|\frac{\partial^{2}\mathsf{r}_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}(s)}{\partial\alpha^{2}}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right|\leq\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left\|\frac{\partial^{2}\mathsf{r}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}{\partial\theta(s,\cdot)^{2}}\right\|_{2}\left\|u\right\|_{2}^{2}\kern 5.0pt,

where in the last inequality, we used the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the definition of the matrix operator norm.. We now compute the second derivative of
\mathsf{r}_{\theta}^{f}(s)
. Starting from (
48
), we get

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}\mathsf{r}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}{\partial\theta(s,b)\partial\theta(s,c)}=-\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(b|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(b|s)^{2}}\left[\mathsf{1}_{c}(b)\frac{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(b|s\right)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(b|s\right)}-\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(c|s)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(b|s\right)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(b|s\right)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(c|s\right)}\cdot\frac{1}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\right]\mathsf{r}(s,b)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(b|s)f_{\theta}^{\prime}(b|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(b|s)^{2}}\left[\mathsf{1}_{c}(b)\frac{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(b|s\right)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(b|s\right)}-\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(c|s)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(b|s\right)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(b|s\right)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(c|s\right)}\cdot\frac{1}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda\left[\mathsf{1}_{b}(c)\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(b|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(b|s)}-\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(b|s)\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(c|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(b|s)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(c|s)}\cdot\frac{1}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(b|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(a|s)^{2}f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(b|s)}\cdot\frac{\mathsf{r}(s,a)}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\left[\mathsf{1}_{c}(a)\frac{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(a|s\right)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(a|s\right)}-\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(c|s)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(a|s\right)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(a|s\right)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(c|s\right)}\cdot\frac{1}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(b|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(a|s)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(b|s)^{2}}\cdot\frac{\mathsf{r}(s,a)}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\left[\mathsf{1}_{c}(b)\frac{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(b|s\right)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(b|s\right)}-\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(c|s)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(b|s\right)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(b|s\right)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(c|s\right)}\cdot\frac{1}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(b|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(a|s)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(b|s)}\cdot\frac{\mathsf{r}(s,a)}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)^{2}}\left[-\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(c|s)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}(c|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(c|s)^{3}}+\frac{1}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\sum_{d\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(c|s)\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(d|s)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(d|s\right)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(d|s\right)^{3}f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(c|s\right)}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(b|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(a|s)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(b|s)}\frac{f_{\theta}^{\prime}(b|s)}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)^{2}}\left[-\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(c|s)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}(c|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(c|s)^{3}}+\frac{1}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\sum_{d\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(c|s)\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(d|s)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(d|s\right)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(d|s\right)^{3}f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(c|s\right)}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(b|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(a|s)^{2}f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(b|s)}\frac{f_{\theta}^{\prime}(b|s)}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\left[\mathsf{1}_{c}(a)\frac{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(a|s\right)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(a|s\right)}-\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(c|s)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(a|s\right)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(a|s\right)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(c|s\right)}\cdot\frac{1}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(b|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(a|s)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(b|s)^{2}}\frac{f_{\theta}^{\prime}(b|s)}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\left[\mathsf{1}_{c}(b)\frac{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(b|s\right)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(b|s\right)}-\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(c|s)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(b|s\right)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(b|s\right)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(c|s\right)}\cdot\frac{1}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(a|s)}\frac{1}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\left[\mathsf{1}_{b}(c)\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(b|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(b|s)}-\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(b|s)\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(c|s)}{f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(b|s)f_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}(c|s)}\cdot\frac{1}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Taking the absolute value, applying the triangle inequality, using that under
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
, for all
x\in\mathbb{R}{+}
, we have
|f^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)/f^{\prime\prime}(x)^{2}|\leq\kappa_{f}
, and using that the rewards are bounded by
1
gives

\displaystyle\sum_{b\in\mathcal{A}}\sum_{c\in\mathcal{A}}\left|\frac{\partial^{2}\mathsf{r}_{\theta_{0}}^{f}(s)}{\partial\theta(s,b)\partial\theta(s,c)}\right|\leq 8\kappa_{f}\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)+8\lambda\kappa_{f}\operatorname{Y}_{\theta}^{f}(s)+4\lambda\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\kern 5.0pt,

which concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma B.11
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
and
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
respectively.
Then, for any
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
and
u\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
,

\displaystyle\left|u^{\top}\frac{\partial^{2}v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s)}{\partial\theta^{2}}u\right|\leq\left(\sum_{i=1}^{3}\frac{L^{(i)}_{\lambda,f}(\theta)}{(1-\gamma)^{i}}\right)\left\|u\right\|_{2}^{2}\kern 5.0pt,

where for
i\in\{1,2,3\},L^{(i)}_{\lambda,f}(\theta)
, are defined as

\displaystyle L^{(1)}_{\lambda,f}(\theta)

\displaystyle:=4(2\kappa_{f}+\lambda)\left\|\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}+8\lambda\kappa_{f}\left\|\operatorname{Y}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle L^{(2)}_{\lambda,f}(\theta)

\displaystyle:=8\gamma\left\|\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}\left(\kappa_{f}\{1+\lambda\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\operatorname{D}^{f}({\pi_{\theta}^{f}}(\cdot|s)\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))\}+\left\|\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}+\lambda\left\|\operatorname{Y}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle L^{(3)}_{\lambda,f}(\theta)

\displaystyle:=8\gamma^{2}\left\|\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}(1+\lambda\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\operatorname{D}^{f}({\pi_{\theta}^{f}}(\cdot|s)\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

By construction, we get

\displaystyle\left|u^{\top}\frac{\partial^{2}v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s)}{\partial\theta^{2}}u\right|=\left|\frac{\partial^{2}v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}(s)}{\partial\alpha^{2}}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right|\kern 5.0pt.

Using (
47
), we get that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial^{2}v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}(s)}{\partial\alpha^{2}}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\left|2\gamma^{2}\mathbf{e}_{s}^{\top}M(0)\frac{\partial\mathsf{P}_{\theta_{\alpha}}}{\partial\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}M(0)\frac{\partial\mathsf{P}_{\theta_{\alpha}}}{\partial\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}M(0)\mathsf{r}_{\theta}^{f}\right|}_{\mathbf{(A)}}+\underbrace{\left|\gamma\mathbf{e}_{s}^{\top}M(0)\frac{\partial^{2}\mathsf{P}_{\theta_{\alpha}}}{\partial^{2}\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}M(0)\mathsf{r}_{\theta}^{f}\right|}_{\mathbf{(B)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\left|2\gamma\mathbf{e}_{s}^{\top}M(0)\frac{\partial\mathsf{P}_{\theta_{\alpha}}}{\partial\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}M(0)\frac{\partial\mathsf{r}_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}}{\partial\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right|}_{\mathbf{(C)}}+\underbrace{\left|\mathbf{e}_{s}^{\top}M(0)\frac{\partial^{2}\mathsf{r}_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}}{\partial^{2}\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right|}_{\mathbf{(D)}}\kern 5.0pt.

We now bound each of these terms separately

Bounding
\mathbf{(A)}
.

First note that, for any vector
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{S}}
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\|M(\alpha)x\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\|x\|_{\infty}\kern 5.0pt,

(49)

This yields

\displaystyle\mathbf{(A)}\leq 2\gamma^{2}\left\|M(0)\frac{\partial\mathsf{P}_{\theta_{\alpha}}}{\partial\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}M(0)\frac{\partial\mathsf{P}_{\theta_{\alpha}}}{\partial\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}M(0)\mathsf{r}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{2\gamma^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}\left\|\frac{\partial\mathsf{P}_{\theta_{\alpha}}}{\partial\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\mathsf{r}_{\theta}^{f}\|_{\infty}

By using again (
49
),
Lemma

B.9
, and
Lemma

B.10
we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{(A)}\leq\frac{8\gamma^{2}\max_{s}\{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\}^{2}\left\|u\right\|_{2}^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}(1+\lambda\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\operatorname{D}^{f}({\pi_{\theta}^{f}}(\cdot|s)\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s)))\kern 5.0pt.

Bounding
\mathbf{(B)}
.

Using (
49
),
Lemma

B.9
, and
Lemma

B.10
we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{(B)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left\|\frac{\partial^{2}\mathsf{P}_{\theta_{\alpha}}}{\partial^{2}\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right\|_{\infty}\|\mathsf{r}_{\theta}^{f}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\gamma}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\kappa_{f}\max_{s}\{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\}\left\|u\right\|_{2}^{2}\{1+\lambda\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\operatorname{D}^{f}({\pi_{\theta}^{f}}(\cdot|s)\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))\}\kern 5.0pt.

Bounding
\mathbf{(C)}
.

Similarly, using (
49
),
Lemma

B.9
, and
Lemma

B.10
we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{(C)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\gamma}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\}\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left\{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)+\lambda\operatorname{Y}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\right\}\left\|u\right\|_{2}^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

Bounding
\mathbf{(D)}
.

Using (
49
),
Lemma

B.9
, and
Lemma

B.10
we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{(D)}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\left\|\frac{\partial^{2}\mathsf{r}_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{f}}{\partial^{2}\alpha}\bigg|_{\alpha=0}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left\{4(2\kappa_{f}+\lambda)\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)+8\lambda\kappa_{f}\operatorname{Y}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\right\}\left\|u\right\|_{2}^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

The proof is completed by collecting these upper bounds.
∎

Theorem B.12
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
and
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
respectively. Then for any
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left|v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta^{\prime}}^{f}(\rho)-v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)-\langle\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)}{\partial\theta},\theta^{\prime}-\theta\rangle\right|\leq\frac{L_{f}}{2}\left\|\theta^{\prime}-\theta\right\|_{2}^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

where

\displaystyle L_{f}:=\frac{8\omega_{f}\left(\gamma\omega_{f}+(1-\gamma)\kappa_{f}\right)}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}+4\lambda\frac{2\gamma^{2}\omega_{f}^{2}\mathrm{d}_{f}+2\gamma(1-\gamma)\omega_{f}\left[\kappa_{f}\mathrm{d}_{f}+\mathrm{y}_{f}\right]+(1-\gamma)^{2}\left[\omega_{f}+2\kappa_{f}\mathrm{y}_{f}\right]}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

Fix any vector
u\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
and
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
.Using
Lemma

B.11
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left|u^{\top}\frac{\partial^{2}v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s)}{\partial\theta^{2}}u\right|\leq\left(\sum_{i=1}^{3}\frac{L^{(i)}_{\lambda,f}(\theta)}{(1-\gamma)^{i}}\right)\left\|u\right\|_{2}^{2}\kern 5.0pt,

where for
i\in\{1,2,3\},L^{(i)}_{\lambda,f}(\theta)
, are defined as

\displaystyle L^{(1)}_{\lambda,f}(\theta)

\displaystyle:=4(2\kappa_{f}+\lambda)\left\|\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}+8\lambda\kappa_{f}\left\|\operatorname{Y}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle L^{(2)}_{\lambda,f}(\theta)

\displaystyle:=8\gamma\left\|\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}\left(\kappa_{f}\{1+\lambda\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\operatorname{D}^{f}({\pi_{\theta}^{f}}(\cdot|s)\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))\}+\left\|\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}+\lambda\left\|\operatorname{Y}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle L^{(3)}_{\lambda,f}(\theta)

\displaystyle:=8\gamma^{2}\left\|\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}(1+\lambda\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\operatorname{D}^{f}({\pi_{\theta}^{f}}(\cdot|s)\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))\kern 5.0pt.

Using
Lemma

B.6
combined with
Lemma

I.1
concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix C
Non-Uniform Łojasiewicz inequality

Firstly, define respectively
q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}
and
d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}
as the regularized Q-function and discounted state visitation associated with the policy
\pi_{\theta}^{f}
, i.e.

\displaystyle q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,a)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{r}(s,a)+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\mathsf{P}(s^{\prime}|s,a)v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s^{\prime})\kern 5.0pt,

(50)

\displaystyle d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}(s)

\displaystyle=(1-\gamma)\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\rho\mathsf{P}_{\pi_{\theta}^{f}}^{t}(s)\kern 5.0pt.

(51)

The goal of this section is to prove that the global objective satisfies a non-uniform Łojasiewicz inequality, i.e we aim to show the following theorem

Theorem C.1
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
and
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
respectively. Assume in addition that the initial distribution
\rho
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{\rho}
. Then, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)}{\partial\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq\mu_{f}(\theta)\left(v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)\right)\kern 5.0pt,

where

\displaystyle\mu_{f}(\theta):=\frac{\lambda(1-\gamma)\rho_{\min}^{2}\zeta_{f}^{2}}{\omega_{f}^{2}}\min_{(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

One of the main challenges in establishing such an inequality lies in connecting global information (the suboptimality gap) to local information (the gradient norm). Recall from
Section

2
that if

\theta(s,a)=q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(s,a)/\lambda,\quad\forall\,a\in\mathcal{A},

then
\pi_{\theta}^{f}=\pi_{\star}^{f}
. This observation highlights that, under this parameterization and regularization, the key quantity is the closeness between
\theta
and
q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}/\lambda
.
Formally, we will show that both the suboptimality gap and the gradient norm can be upper and lower bounded, respectively, by a quantity proportional to
\|\zeta_{\theta}(s)\|_{2}
, where we define

\displaystyle\zeta_{\theta}^{f}(s)

\displaystyle:=q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,\cdot)/\lambda-\,\theta(s,\cdot)-K_{\theta}^{f}(s)\,\mathsf{1}_{|\mathcal{A}|},

(52)

\displaystyle K_{\theta}^{f}(s)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\langle q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,\cdot)/\lambda-\theta(s,\cdot),\mathsf{1}_{|\mathcal{A}|}\rangle}{|\mathcal{A}|}\kern 5.0pt.

(53)

Note that
\zeta^{f}_{\theta}
is the projection of
q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,\cdot)/\lambda-\theta(s,\cdot)
onto the subspace orthogonal to
\mathsf{1}_{|\mathcal{A}|}
.

The proof proceeds in three steps:

1.

Derive an explicit expression for the gradient of the objective and establish a lower bound in terms of
\|\zeta_{\theta}^{f}\|
.

2.

Upper bound the suboptimality gap by a quantity directly related to
\|\zeta_{\theta}^{f}\|
.

3.

Combine these two bounds to identify the corresponding non-uniform PL coefficient.

We now detail each step in turn.

C.1
Lower Bounding the norm of the gradient

Before deriving a lower bound on the norm of the gradient, we start by deriving an expression for the latter.

Lemma C.2
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
and
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
respectively. For any
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
b\in\mathcal{A}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}=\frac{d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}(s)}{1-\gamma}

\displaystyle\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(b|s)\left[q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,b)-\lambda\theta(s,b)-\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)\left[q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,a)-\lambda\theta(s,a)\right]\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

Fix
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
b\in\mathcal{A}
. Additionally fix any
\tilde{s}\in\mathcal{S}
. Using (
45
), we have

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\tilde{s})

\displaystyle=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|\tilde{s})\mathsf{r}(\tilde{s},a)-\lambda\operatorname{D}^{f}({\pi_{\theta}^{f}}(\cdot|\tilde{s})\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|\tilde{s}))+\gamma\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\sum_{\tilde{s}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|\tilde{s})\mathsf{P}(\tilde{s}^{\prime}|\tilde{s},a)v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\tilde{s}^{\prime})

Deriving the preceding recursion with respect to
\theta(s,b)
yields

\displaystyle\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\tilde{s})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\partial\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|\tilde{s})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\left[\mathsf{r}(\tilde{s},a)-\lambda f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|\tilde{s})}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|\tilde{s})}\right)+\gamma\sum_{\tilde{s}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\mathsf{P}(\tilde{s}^{\prime}|\tilde{s},a)v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\tilde{s}^{\prime})\right]}_{Z(\tilde{s})}

\displaystyle\hskip 106.0pt+\gamma\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\sum_{\tilde{s}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|\tilde{s})\mathsf{P}(\tilde{s}^{\prime}|\tilde{s},a)\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\tilde{s}^{\prime})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\kern 5.0pt.

Using the definition of the regularized Q-function and writing the preceding recursion in a vector form yields

\displaystyle\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\cdot)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}=Z(\cdot)+\gamma\mathsf{P}_{\theta}\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\cdot)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\kern 5.0pt.

which implies

\displaystyle\rho^{\top}\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\cdot)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}=\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}=\rho^{\top}(\mathrm{Id}-\gamma\mathsf{P}_{\theta})^{-1}Z(\cdot)\kern 5.0pt.

Next, using the definition of the discounted state visitation (
51
) and the regularized Q-function (
50
) implies

\displaystyle\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}(s^{\prime})\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\partial\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s^{\prime})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\left[q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s^{\prime},a)-\lambda f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s^{\prime})}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(54)

Using that
\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\partial\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}=0
and that for
s\neq s^{\prime}
, we have
\frac{\partial\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s^{\prime})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}=0
yields

\displaystyle\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}(s)\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\partial\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\left[q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,a)-\lambda f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)-\lambda\mu_{\theta}(s)\right]\kern 5.0pt.

where
\mu_{\theta}(s)
is defined in
Corollary

B.7
and satisfies for any
a\in\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle\theta(s,a)-f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)=\mu_{\theta}(s)\kern 5.0pt.

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}(s)\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\partial\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\left[q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,a)-\lambda\theta(s,a)\right]\kern 5.0pt,

Finally, plugging in the expression of the derivative of the policy derived in
Corollary

B.7
in the previous equality concludes the proof.
∎

Using the previous lemma, we prove the following lower bound for the norm of the gradient.

Lemma C.3
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
and
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
respectively. Assume in addition that the initial distribution
\rho
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{\rho}
. We have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)}{\partial\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq\lambda^{2}\rho_{\min}^{2}\min_{(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}}\{\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)^{2}\}\min_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)^{2}\}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left\|\zeta_{\theta}(s)\right\|_{2}^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

It holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)}{\partial\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left\|\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)}{\partial\theta(s,\cdot)}\right\|_{2}^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

Fix
s\in\mathcal{S}
. Using
Lemma

C.2
, we observe that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)}{\partial\theta(s,\cdot)}

\displaystyle=\frac{d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}(s)}{1-\gamma}H(\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|s))\left[q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,\cdot)-\lambda\theta(s,\cdot)\right]\kern 5.0pt.

where for any vector
u\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{A}|}
, we define
H(u):=\diag(u)-uu^{\top}
. Thus, we get that

\displaystyle\frac{1-\gamma}{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}\left\|\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)}{\partial\theta(s,\cdot)}\right\|_{2}=d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}(s)\left\|H(\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|s))\left[q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,\cdot)-\lambda\theta(s,\cdot)\right]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\lambda d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}(s)\left\|H(\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|s))\left[q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,\cdot)/\lambda-\theta(s,\cdot)-K_{\theta}^{f}(s)\mathsf{1}_{|\mathcal{A}|}\right]\right\|_{2}\quad\text{(using that $H(u)\mathsf{1}_{|\mathcal{A}|}=0$ and \eqref{def:kparam})}

\displaystyle\quad\geq\lambda d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}(s)\min_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)\left\|\zeta_{\theta}(s)\right\|_{2}\quad\text{(where $\zeta_{\theta}(s)$ is defined in \eqref{def:zeta} and using \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:spectral_norm_H})}\kern 5.0pt.

Finally, using
d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}(s)\geq(1-\gamma)\rho(s)
and
\textbf{A}_{\rho}
concludes the proof.
∎

C.2
Bounding the Suboptimality Gap

The first step is to connect the suboptimality gap to information localized at
\theta
. This is achieved via the performance difference lemma for the regularized value function yields (see
Lemma

I.3
)

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{\lambda d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi_{\star}^{f}}(s)}{1-\gamma}\bigg[\underbrace{\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\pi_{\star}^{f}(a|s)q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,a)/\lambda-\operatorname{D}^{f}(\pi_{\star}^{f}(\cdot|s)\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))-v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s)/\lambda}_{\mathbf{(A(s))}}\bigg].

(55)

Fix
s\in\mathcal{S}
. Using the definition of the regularized value functions and Q-functions combined with
Equation

45
, we have

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s)=\langle\pi_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|s),q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,\cdot)\rangle-\lambda\operatorname{D}^{f}({\pi_{\theta}^{f}}(\cdot|s)\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))\kern 5.0pt.

This implies
\textbf{A}(s)=\textbf{A}_{1}(s)-\textbf{A}_{2}(s)-\textbf{A}_{3}(s)
where

\begin{split}\textbf{A}_{1}(s)&=\langle\pi_{\star}^{f}(\cdot|s),q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,\cdot)/\lambda\rangle-\operatorname{D}^{f}(\pi_{\star}^{f}(\cdot|s)\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))\\
\textbf{A}_{2}(s)&=\langle\pi_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|s),\theta(s,\cdot)\rangle-\operatorname{D}^{f}({\pi_{\theta}^{f}}(\cdot|s)\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))\\
\textbf{A}_{3}(s)&=\langle\pi_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|s),q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,\cdot)/\lambda-\theta(s,\cdot)\rangle\kern 5.0pt.\end{split}

(56)

Using (
4
) and (
18
),
\textbf{A}_{1}(s)\leq\operatorname{f-softmax}(q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,\cdot)/\lambda,\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))
and
\textbf{A}_{2}(s)=\operatorname{f-softmax}(\theta(s,\cdot),\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))
. Thus, we have

\begin{split}\textbf{A}(s)\!&\leq\operatorname{f-softmax}(q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,\cdot)/\lambda,\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))-\operatorname{f-softmax}(\theta(s,\cdot),\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))-\textbf{A}_{3}(s)\\
&=\operatorname{f-softmax}(q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,\cdot)/\lambda,\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))-\operatorname{f-softmax}(\theta(s,\cdot)+K_{\theta}^{f}(s)\mathsf{1}_{|\mathcal{A}|})\\
&-\langle\pi_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|s),q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,\cdot)/\lambda-\theta(s,\cdot)-K_{\theta}^{f}(s)\mathsf{1}_{|\mathcal{A}|}\rangle\kern 5.0pt,\end{split}

(57)

where in the last equality we used that, for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{A}|}
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
,

\operatorname{f-softmax}(x+\alpha\mathsf{1}_{|\mathcal{A}|},\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))=\operatorname{f-softmax}(x,\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))+\alpha\kern 5.0pt.

The structure of
\mathbf{(A(s))}
closely resembles that of a first-order Taylor expansion as by
Lemma

B.5
, we have that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\operatorname{f-softmax}(\theta(s,\cdot),\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))}{\partial\theta(s,\cdot)}=\operatorname{f-softargmax}(\theta(s,\cdot),\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))=\pi_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|s)\kern 5.0pt.

(58)

Lemma C.4
.

Assume
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
. It holds that

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)\leq\frac{\lambda}{1-\gamma}\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}}\{\left\|W_{\theta}\right\|_{\infty}\}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left\|\zeta_{\theta}^{f}(s)\right\|_{2}^{2}

Proof.

In this proof, we denote by
g_{s}(x)=\operatorname{f-softmax}(x,\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))
. Combining (
55
) and (
57
) yields

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)\leq\frac{\lambda}{1-\gamma}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi_{\star}^{f}}(s)B(s)\kern 5.0pt,

(59)

where we have defined

\displaystyle B(s)=\bigg[g_{s}\left(q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,\cdot)/\lambda\right)-g_{s}\left(\theta(s,\cdot)+K_{\theta}^{f}(s)\mathsf{1}_{|\mathcal{A}|}\right)-\langle\pi_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|s),q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,\cdot)/\lambda-\theta(s,\cdot)-K_{\theta}^{f}(s)\mathsf{1}_{|\mathcal{A}|}\rangle\bigg]

Next, by
Lemma

B.5
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial g_{s}(x)}{\partial x}\bigg|_{x=\theta(s,\cdot)+K_{\theta}(s)\mathsf{1}_{|\mathcal{A}|}}=\pi_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|s)\kern 5.0pt.

Standard one-dimensional Taylor theorem with Lagrange remainder shows that there exists
y\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{A}|}
which belongs to the segment between
\theta(s,\cdot)+K_{\theta}(s)\mathsf{1}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
q_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(s,\cdot)/\lambda
such that

\displaystyle B(s)=\frac{1}{2}\langle\frac{\partial^{2}g_{s}(x)}{\partial x^{2}}\bigg|_{x=y}\zeta_{\theta}^{f}(s),\zeta_{\theta}^{f}(s)\rangle

Using the bound on the spectral norm of the Hessian of
g_{s}
derived in
Lemma

B.5
, we obtain

\displaystyle B(s)=\frac{1}{2}\langle\frac{\partial^{2}g_{s}(x)}{\partial x^{2}}\bigg|_{x=y}\zeta_{\theta}^{f},\zeta_{\theta}^{f}\rangle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\frac{\partial^{2}g_{s}(x)}{\partial x^{2}}\bigg|_{x=y}\right\|_{2}\left\|\zeta_{\theta}^{f}(s)\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq\operatorname{W}_{y}^{f}(s)\left\|\zeta_{\theta}^{f}(s)\right\|_{2}^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

Finally, bounding the discounted state visitation measure in (
59
) by
1
and plugging in the preceding bound on
B(s)
concludes the proof.
∎

The proof of
Theorem

C.1
follows immediately from
Lemma

C.3
,
Lemma

C.4
,
Lemma

B.6
, and (
9
).

Appendix D
Monotone Improvement Operators

A key challenge in analyzing stochastic policy gradient methods is that the Łojasiewicz inequality depends on
\theta
and degenerates whenever the probability of an action becomes small. The goal of this section is therefore to show the existence of an operator
\textsc{Imp}^{f}
with two crucial properties:
(i) for any policy, applying this operator produces a new policy with higher objective value, and
(ii) every policy generated by this operator assigns at least a fixed minimum probability to every action. The main idea is to build the improvement operator such that it slightly augments the smallest probability weights, such that for any state action pair
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
the probability ratio
\pi(a|s)/\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)
stays above a certain threshold. We will show below that this procedure improves the global objective while keeping the probabilities uniformly bounded away from
0
when the threshold is properly chosen. Let
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
be such that
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
hold.
For any policy
\pi
, state
s\in\mathcal{S}
,
\tau<\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}/2
, we respectively define
\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)
, and
a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s):=\left\{a\in\mathcal{A},\pi(a|s)/\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\leq\tau/2\right\}\kern 5.0pt,\quad a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)=\argmax_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\{\pi(a|s)/\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\}\kern 5.0pt,

where the
\argmax
is chosen at random in the case of ties.
Note that the definition of
\tau
ensures that
a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)
does not belong to the set
\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)
as

\displaystyle\max_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\pi(a|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\geq 1\kern 5.0pt.

Finally, we define the improvement operator as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{\tau}:\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})^{\mathcal{S}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\;\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})^{\mathcal{S}},

(60)

\displaystyle\pi

\displaystyle\longmapsto\;\mathcal{U}_{\tau}(\pi),

where for every
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
,

\mathcal{U}_{\tau}(\pi)(a|s)\;=\;\begin{cases}\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\tau,&\text{if }\pi(a|s)\leq\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\tau/2,\\[6.0pt]
\pi(a|s)-\displaystyle\sum_{b\in\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)}\bigl(\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(b|s)\tau-\pi(b|s)\bigr),&\text{if }a=a_{\max}^{\pi}(s),\\[10.0pt]
\pi(a|s),&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

The operator
\mathcal{U}_{\tau}
builds
\mathcal{U}_{\tau}(\pi)(a|s)
by (statewise) raising each
a\in\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)
to
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\tau
, substracting the total added mass from the single action
a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)
, and leaving other actions unchanged. If
\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)=\emptyset
, for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
, then
\textsc{Imp}^{f}(\pi)=\pi
. Note that mass conservation is immediate from the definition and the fact that
\tau<\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}/2
. Non-negativity of
\mathcal{U}_{\tau}(\pi)(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)
follows because the removed mass is

\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)}\{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\tau-\pi(a|s)\}\leq\tau\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)}\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\leq\tau

Since
\pi(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)\geq\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)\geq\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}
, and
\tau\leq\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}/2
, we get that
\mathcal{U}_{\tau}(\pi)(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)\geq\tau/2
. This in particular shows that
\mathcal{U}_{\tau}(\pi)
is a policy. As by
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
we have
\lim_{x\rightarrow 0^{+}}f^{\prime}(x)=-\infty
, we consider
[f^{\prime}]^{-1}:(-\infty,f^{\prime}(1/\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})\bigr]\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{+}
the inverse of
f^{\prime}
and define

\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda}:=\min\left([f^{\prime}]^{-1}\left(-\frac{16+8\gamma\lambda\mathrm{d}_{f}}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right),[f^{\prime}]^{-1}\left(-4\left|f^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)\right|\right),\frac{1}{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(61)

The following lemma establishes the crucial improvement property when
\tau=\tau_{\lambda}
.

Lemma D.1
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
and
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
respectively. Assume in addition that the initial distribution
\rho
satisfies
\textbf{A}_{\rho}
. For any policy
\pi
, it holds that

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\mathcal{U}_{\tau_{\lambda}}(\pi)}^{f}(\rho)\geq v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}^{f}(\rho)\kern 5.0pt.

Additionally, for any policy
\pi
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{\tau_{\lambda}}(\pi)(a|s)\geq\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}\tau_{\lambda}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

Set an arbitrary policy
\pi
. For avoiding heavy notations, we will, through this proof, denote by
A_{\tau}^{\pi}=A_{\tau_{\lambda}}^{\pi}
. We consider the case where there is
s\in\mathcal{S}
such that
\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)\neq\emptyset
(alternatively
\mathcal{U}_{\tau_{\lambda}}(\pi)=\pi
, which makes the previous inequality immediately valid). Define
\tilde{\pi}=\mathcal{U}_{\tau_{\lambda}}(\pi)
. The following applies

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\tilde{\pi}}^{f}(\rho)

\displaystyle-v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}^{f}(\rho)=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\tilde{\pi}}(s)\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\left[\tilde{\pi}(a|s)\mathsf{r}(s,a)-\lambda\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)f\left(\frac{\tilde{\pi}(a|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 72.0pt-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}(s)\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\left[\pi(a|s)\mathsf{r}(s,a)-\lambda\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)f\left(\frac{\pi(a|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left(d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\tilde{\pi}}(s)-d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}(s)\right)\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\left[\tilde{\pi}(a|s)\mathsf{r}(s,a)-\lambda\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)f\left(\frac{\tilde{\pi}(a|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)\right]}_{\mathbf{(I)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}(s)\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{\pi}(a|s)-\pi(a|s))\mathsf{r}(s,a)}_{\mathbf{(II)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\lambda\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}(s)\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\left[f\left(\frac{\pi(a|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)-f\left(\frac{\tilde{\pi}(a|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)\right]}_{\mathbf{(III)}}\kern 5.0pt.

We now lower-bound each of the three terms separately.

Bounding
\mathbf{(I)}
.

Using
Lemma

I.2
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{(I)}

\displaystyle\geq-\left\|d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\tilde{\pi}}-d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}\right\|_{1}\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left|\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\left[\tilde{\pi}(a|s)\mathsf{r}(s,a)-\lambda\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)f\left(\frac{\tilde{\pi}(a|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\sup_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left\|\tilde{\pi}(\cdot|s)-\pi(\cdot|s)\right\|_{1}\sup_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left[1+\lambda\operatorname{D}^{f}(\tilde{\pi}(\cdot|s)\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))\right]

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{2\gamma}{1-\gamma}\tau_{\lambda}\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left\{\sum_{a\in A_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)}\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\right\}\sup_{\nu\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})}\sup_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left[1+\lambda\operatorname{D}^{f}(\nu\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))\right]\kern 5.0pt,

where in the last inequality we used that (because we increase the probability of the actions in
A_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)
by
\tau_{\lambda}
and remove the total added mass from the probability of
\pi(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)
)

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left\|\tilde{\pi}(\cdot|s)-\pi(\cdot|s)\right\|_{1}\leq 2\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left\{\sum_{a\in A_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)}\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\right\}\tau_{\lambda}\kern 5.0pt.

Bounding
\mathbf{(II)}
.

Using the triangle inequality yields

\displaystyle\mathbf{(II)}\geq-\sup_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left\|\tilde{\pi}(\cdot|s)-\pi(\cdot|s)\right\|_{1}\geq-2\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left\{\sum_{a\in A_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)}\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\right\}\tau_{\lambda}\kern 5.0pt.

Bounding
\mathbf{(III)}
.

All the state-action pairs on which the original
\pi
allocates the same probability then the policy
\tilde{\pi}
are equal to
0
in
\mathbf{(III)}
allowing us to simplify this term

\displaystyle\mathbf{(III)}

\displaystyle=\lambda\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}(s)\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\left[f\left(\frac{\pi(a|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)-f\left(\frac{\tilde{\pi}(a|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\lambda\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}(s)\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)}\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\left[f\left(\frac{\pi(a|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)-f\left(\frac{\tilde{\pi}(a|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\lambda\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathsf{1}(\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)\neq\emptyset)d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}(s)\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)\left[f\left(\frac{\pi(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)}\right)-f\left(\frac{\tilde{\pi}(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)}\right)\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Since
f
is convex, for all
u,v\in[0;1/\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}]
,
f(u)-f(v)\geq f^{\prime}(v)(u-v)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{(III)}

\displaystyle\geq\lambda\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}(s)\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)}(\pi(a|s)-\tilde{\pi}(a|s))f^{\prime}(\tau_{\lambda})\qquad\text{(since $\tilde{\pi}(a|s)/\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)=\tau_{\lambda}$)}

\displaystyle+\lambda\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathsf{1}(\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)\neq\emptyset)d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}(s)\left[\pi(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)-\tilde{\pi}(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)\right]f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\tilde{\pi}(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

Next, using that

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{\pi}(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)}\geq\frac{\pi(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)-\tau_{\lambda}}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)}\geq\frac{\pi(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)-\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}/2}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)}\geq 1-\frac{1}{2}=\frac{1}{2}\kern 5.0pt,

combined with the monotonicity of
f^{\prime}
and the fact that
\pi(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)-\tilde{\pi}(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)\geq 0
yields

\displaystyle\mathbf{(III)}

\displaystyle\geq\lambda\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}(s)\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)}(\pi(a|s)-\tilde{\pi}(a|s))f^{\prime}(\tau_{\lambda})\qquad\text{(since $\tilde{\pi}(a|s)/\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)=\tau_{\lambda}$)}

\displaystyle+\lambda\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathsf{1}(\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)\neq\emptyset)d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}(s)\left[\pi(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)-\tilde{\pi}(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)\right]f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\tilde{\pi}(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

Additionally, since

\displaystyle 0\leq\pi(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)-\tilde{\pi}(a_{\max}^{\pi}(s)|s)\leq\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)}(\pi(a|s)-\tilde{\pi}(a|s))\leq\tau_{\lambda}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)}\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\kern 5.0pt,

implies

\displaystyle\mathbf{(III)}

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{\lambda}{2}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}(s)\mathsf{1}(\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)\neq\emptyset)\left(\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)}\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\right)\tau_{\lambda}f^{\prime}(\tau_{\lambda})

\displaystyle+\lambda\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}(s)\mathsf{1}(\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)\neq\emptyset)\left(\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)}\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\right)\tau_{\lambda}f^{\prime}\left(1/2\right)\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{\lambda}{4}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}d_{\rho}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}(s)\mathsf{1}(\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)\neq\emptyset)\left(\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)}\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\right)\tau_{\lambda}f^{\prime}\left(\tau_{\lambda}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

where in the last inequality, we used that
f^{\prime}(\tau_{\lambda})\leq-4|f^{\prime}(1/2)|
. Hence, by using
\textbf{A}_{\rho}
, we can lower bound this term as follows

\displaystyle\mathbf{(III)}\geq-\frac{\lambda}{4}(1-\gamma)\min_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\{\rho(s)\}\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left\{\sum_{a\in A_{\tau}^{\pi}(s)}\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\right\}\tau_{\lambda}f^{\prime}(\tau_{\lambda})\kern 5.0pt.

Collecting these lower bounds and using that

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(\tau_{\lambda})\leq-\frac{16+8\gamma\lambda\mathrm{d}_{f}}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}

concludes the proof.
∎
Finally, we define the operator that maps each policy to one corresponding parameter

M^{f}\colon\Pi\;\to\;\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}

by

\displaystyle M^{f}(\pi)(s,a)\;:=\;f^{\prime}\!\left(\frac{\pi(a\mid s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a\mid s)}\right)-f^{\prime}\!\left(\frac{\pi(a_{|\mathcal{A}|}|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{|\mathcal{A}|}|s)}\right),\quad\forall(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\,.

(62)

Finally, we define the improvement operator on the logitspace as

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\tau}:=M^{f}\circ\mathcal{U}_{\tau}\kern 5.0pt.

The following lemma shows that
M^{f}
successfully recovers a parameter that gives the policy and that
\mathcal{T}_{\tau}
improves the value of the objective when
\lambda=\tau_{\lambda}
.

Lemma D.2
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
and
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
respectively. Assume in addition that the initial distribution
\rho
satisfies
\textbf{A}_{\rho}
. For any policy
\pi
, it holds that

\displaystyle\pi_{{M^{f}(\pi)}}^{f}=\pi\kern 5.0pt,

Additionally, for any
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
and
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
, we have that

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\mathcal{T}_{\tau_{\lambda}}(\theta)}^{f}\geq v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}\kern 5.0pt,\quad\pi_{\mathcal{T}_{\tau_{\lambda}}(\theta)}^{f}\geq\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}\tau_{\lambda}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

The proof follows immediately from a combination of equality (
43
) in
Corollary

B.7
, (
62
), and
Lemma

D.1
.
∎

Appendix E
Convergence analysis of Stochastic Policy Gradient

In this section, we aim to derive under
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and
\textbf{A}_{\rho}
non-asymptotic convergence rates for
f
-PG
. First, we establish a bound on the bias and variance of the REINFORCE estimator defined in (
13
).

E.1
Bounding the bias and variance of the stochastic estimator

First, recall the expression of the stochastic estimator of the gradient

\begin{split}\mathrm{g}_{z}^{f}(\theta)&=\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=0}^{B-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{h}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{\ell}|s_{\ell})}{\partial\theta}\gamma^{h}\mathsf{r}(s_{h},a_{h})\\
&-\lambda\cdot\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=0}^{B-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{h-1}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{\ell}|s_{\ell})}{\partial\theta}\gamma^{h}\operatorname{D}^{f}({\pi_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|s_{h})}\|{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s_{h})})-\lambda\cdot\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=0}^{B-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\mathrm{F}_{\theta}^{f}(s_{h})\kern 5.0pt,\end{split}

(63)

where
z=(s_{0:H-1}^{b},a_{0:H-1}^{b})_{b=0}^{B-1}\in(\mathcal{S}\cdot\mathcal{A})^{H\cdot B}
, and we recall that for any
s\in\mathcal{S}
,
\mathrm{F}_{\theta}^{f}(s)
is a vector of size
|\mathcal{S}|\times|\mathcal{A}|
defined in (
14
) as

\displaystyle[\mathrm{F}_{\theta}^{f}(s)]_{(s^{\prime},b)}=\mathsf{1}_{s}(s^{\prime})\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(b|s)\left[f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(b|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(b|s)}\right)-\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)\right]\kern 5.0pt,

and where
\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}
,
\operatorname{Y}_{\theta}^{f}
, and
\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}
are defined in (
41
) and (
42
).
Finally, define the expected gradient estimator as

\mathrm{g}^{f}(\theta):=\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim[\nu(\theta)]^{\otimes B}}\left[\mathrm{g}_{Z}^{f}(\theta)\right]\kern 5.0pt,

(64)

Before bounding the bias and the variance, we give an explicit expression of the derivative of the log probability that appears in the expression of our stochastic gradient estimator. We also provide a bound on the derivative of the log probabilities and on the matrix
\mathrm{F}_{\theta}^{f}(s)
for any state
s\in\mathcal{S}
.

Lemma E.1
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
. For any
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
,
(s,s^{\prime},a,b)\in\mathcal{S}^{2}\times\mathcal{A}^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial\theta(s^{\prime},b)}=\mathsf{1}_{s^{\prime}}(s)\frac{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}\left[\mathsf{1}_{b}(a)\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)-\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(b|s)\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Additionally, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\partial\theta}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{2\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}\kern 5.0pt,\quad\left\|\mathrm{F}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\right\|_{2}\leq 2\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\operatorname{Y}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

The proof follows from the log-derivative trick and the expression of the derivative of the policy provided in
Corollary

B.7
.
∎

Next, we establish a REINFORCE-type formula for the gradient of the objective.

Lemma E.2
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
. It holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\gamma^{t}\mathsf{r}(S_{t},A_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\gamma^{t}\operatorname{D}^{f}({\pi_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|S_{t})}\|{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|S_{t})})\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\mathsf{1}_{s}(S_{t})\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(s)\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(b|s)\left[f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(b|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(b|s)}\right)-\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)\right]\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

Fix a parameter
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
, a horizon
T
, and a divergence generator
f
. For any truncated trajectory
z=(s_{t},a_{t})_{t=0}^{T-1}\in(\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A})^{T}
, we define its probability as

\displaystyle\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta;z)\;=\;\rho(s_{0})\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{0}|s_{0})\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathsf{P}(s_{t}|s_{t-1},a_{t-1})\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{t}|s_{t})\kern 5.0pt,

and the regularized return

\displaystyle R_{\theta,T}^{f}(z)\;=\;\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\gamma^{t}\Big(\mathsf{r}(s_{t},a_{t})-\lambda\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{t}|s_{t})}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{t}|s_{t})}f\big(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{t}|s_{t})}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{t}|s_{t})}\big)\Big).

(65)

The finite-horizon objective is

\displaystyle J_{T}^{f}(\theta)=\sum_{z\in(\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A})^{T}}\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta;z)R_{\theta,T}^{f}(z)\kern 5.0pt.

Fix
(s,b)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
. Differentiating this finite-horizon objective gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial J_{T}^{f}(\theta)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}=\underbrace{\sum_{z\in(\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A})^{T}}\frac{\partial\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta;z)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}R_{\theta,T}^{f}(z)}_{\mathbf{(A)}}+\underbrace{\sum_{z\in(\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A})^{T}}\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta;z)\frac{\partial R_{\theta,T}^{f}(z)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}}_{\mathbf{(B)}}\kern 5.0pt.

(66)

We now treat these two terms separately.

Term
\mathbf{(A)}
.

Using the log-derivative trick and the fact that the only terms that depend on
\theta
in
\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta;z)
are the ones that depend on the policy itself, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta;z)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}=\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta;z)\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{t}|s_{t})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\kern 5.0pt.

(67)

Plugging expressions (
65
) and (
67
) in
\mathbf{(A)}
then gives

\displaystyle\mathbf{(A)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\in(\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A})^{T}}\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta;z)\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{T-1}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{\ell}|s_{\ell})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\gamma^{t}\Big(\mathsf{r}(s_{t},a_{t})-\lambda\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{t}|s_{t})}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{t}|s_{t})}f\bigg(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{t}|s_{t})}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{t}|s_{t})}\bigg)\Big)\kern 5.0pt.

Now observe that for
\ell>t
, the sum of the log-gradient derivatives over
z\in(\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A})^{T}
is
0
. Therefore
\mathbf{(A)}
reduces to

\displaystyle\mathbf{(A)}=\sum_{z\in(\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A})^{T}}\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta;z)\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{\ell}|s_{\ell})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\gamma^{t}\Big(\mathsf{r}(s_{t},a_{t})-\lambda\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{t}|s_{t})}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{t}|s_{t})}f\bigg(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{t}|s_{t})}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{t}|s_{t})}\bigg)\Big)\kern 5.0pt.

(68)

Term
\mathbf{(B)}
.

Taking the derivative of (
65
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial R_{\theta,T}^{f}(z)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}=\lambda\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\gamma^{t}\Big(\frac{\partial\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{t}|s_{t})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{t}|s_{t})}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{t}|s_{t})^{2}}f\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{t}|s_{t})}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{t}|s_{t})}\right)-\frac{\partial\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{t}|s_{t})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\frac{1}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{t}|s_{t})}f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{t}|s_{t})}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{t}|s_{t})}\right)\Big)\kern 5.0pt.

Summing over
z\in(\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A})^{T}
and using the log-derivative trick gives

\displaystyle\mathbf{(B)}=\lambda\!\!\sum_{z\in(\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A})^{T}}\!\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta;z)\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\gamma^{t}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{t}|s_{t})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\left[\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{t}|s_{t})}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{t}|s_{t})}f\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{t}|s_{t})}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{t}|s_{t})}\right)-f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{t}|s_{t})}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a_{t}|s_{t})}\right)\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(69)

Plugging the expressions (
68
) and (
69
) in (
66
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial J_{T}^{f}(\theta)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta)}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\gamma^{t}\left(\mathsf{r}(S_{t},A_{t})-\lambda\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(A_{t}|S_{t})}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{t}|S_{t})}f\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{t}|S_{t})}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(A_{t}|S_{t})}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda\,\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta)}\!\left[\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\gamma^{t}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{t}|S_{t})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\left(\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(A_{t}|S_{t})}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{t}|S_{t})}f\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{t}|S_{t})}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(A_{t}|S_{t})}\right)-f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{t}|S_{t})}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(A_{t}|S_{t})}\right)\right)\right]\kern 5.0pt.

The previous term can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\frac{\partial J_{T}^{f}(\theta)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta)}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\gamma^{t}\mathsf{r}(S_{t},A_{t})\right]

\displaystyle-\lambda\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta)}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\gamma^{t}\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(A_{t}|S_{t})}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{t}|S_{t})}f\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{t}|S_{t})}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(A_{t}|S_{t})}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda\,\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta)}\!\left[\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\gamma^{t}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{t}|S_{t})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{t}|S_{t})}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(A_{t}|S_{t})}\right)\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Applying the tower property by taking the conditional expectation with respect to
\mathcal{G}_{t}:=\sigma(S_{0},A_{0},\dots,S_{t})
on the second expectation and using
Lemma

E.1
in the third expectation, gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial J_{T}^{f}(\theta)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta)}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\gamma^{t}\mathsf{r}(S_{t},A_{t})\right]

\displaystyle-\lambda\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta)}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\gamma^{t}\operatorname{D}^{f}({\pi_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|S_{t})}\|{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|S_{t})})\right]

\displaystyle-\lambda\,\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta)}\!\left[\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\gamma^{t}\mathsf{1}_{s}(S_{t})\frac{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(S_{t})\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(b|S_{t})}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{t}|S_{t})}\left[\mathsf{1}_{b}(A_{t})-\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(A_{t}|S_{t})\right]f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{t}|S_{t})}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(A_{t}|S_{t})}\right)\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Applying the tower property again by taking the conditional expectation with respect to
\mathcal{G}_{t}
in the third expectation gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial J_{T}^{f}(\theta)}{\partial\theta(s,b)}=\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta)}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\gamma^{t}\mathsf{r}(S_{t},A_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta)}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}{\partial\theta(s,b)}\gamma^{t}\operatorname{D}^{f}({\pi_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|S_{t})}\|{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|S_{t})})\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda\,\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim\nu_{T}^{f}(\theta)}\!\left[\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\gamma^{t}\mathsf{1}_{s}(S_{t})\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(S_{t})\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(b|S_{t})\left[f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(b|S_{t})}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(b|S_{t})}\right)-\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|S_{t})f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|S_{t})}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|S_{t})}\right)\right]\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Taking
T\rightarrow+\infty
and applying the dominated convergence theorem concludes the proof.
∎
The following lemma establishes a bound on the variance and bias of the stochastic estimator.

Lemma E.3
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
.
There exists a constant
\beta_{f}\geq 0
such that, for any parameter
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{g}^{f}(\theta)-\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)}{\partial\theta}\right\|_{2}\leq\beta_{f}\kern 5.0pt,

where
\beta_{f}
is an upper bound on the bias defined as

\displaystyle\beta_{f}

\displaystyle:=\frac{2\gamma^{H}(H+1)}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\omega_{f}\left[2+2\lambda\mathrm{d}_{f}+\lambda(1-\gamma)\mathrm{y}_{f}\right]\kern 5.0pt,

where
\omega_{f}
is defined in
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and
\mathrm{d}_{f}
, and
\mathrm{y}_{f}
are defined in (
7
).

Proof.

Using the expression of the gradient truncated at
H
from (
63
) and (
64
), of the true gradient from
Lemma

E.2
, and the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{g}^{f}(\theta)-\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)}{\partial\theta}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=H}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t}\gamma^{t}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}^{\pi_{\theta}^{f}}\left[\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}{\partial\theta}\mathsf{r}(S_{t},A_{t})\right]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{t=H}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\lambda\gamma^{t}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}^{\pi_{\theta}^{f}}\left[\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}{\partial\theta}\operatorname{D}^{f}({\pi_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|S_{t})}\|{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|S_{t})})\right]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda\sum_{t=H}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}^{\pi_{\theta}^{f}}\Big[\mathrm{F}_{\theta}^{f}(S_{t})\Big]\right\|_{2}\kern 5.0pt.

Next, applying
Lemma

E.1
combined with the triangle inequality yields

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{g}^{f}(\theta)-\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)}{\partial\theta}\right\|_{2}\leq\sum_{t=H}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t}\gamma^{t}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}^{\pi_{\theta}^{f}}\left[\frac{2\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(S_{\ell})\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}\left|\mathsf{r}(S_{t},A_{t})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{t=H}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\lambda\gamma^{t}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}^{\pi_{\theta}^{f}}\left[\frac{2\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(S_{\ell})\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}\operatorname{D}^{f}({\pi_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|S_{t})}\|{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|S_{t})})\right]+\lambda\sum_{t=H}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}^{\pi_{\theta}^{f}}\Big[2\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(S_{t})\operatorname{Y}_{\theta}^{f}(S_{t})\Big]\kern 5.0pt.

We define the following filtration, for
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{t}=\sigma(S_{0},A_{0},\dots,S_{t})\kern 5.0pt,

Next, applying the tower property of the conditional expectation by conditioning on
\mathcal{G}_{t}
, bounding the reward and the divergence respectively by 1, and
\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sup_{\nu\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})}\operatorname{D}^{f}(\nu\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))\}
, and using that
\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|s)\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})
for any
s\in\mathcal{S}
yields

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{g}^{f}(\theta)-\frac{\partial v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\theta}^{f}(\rho)}{\partial\theta}\right\|_{2}\leq 2\sum_{t=H}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t}\gamma^{t}\left\|\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad+2\lambda\sum_{t=H}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\gamma^{t}\left\|\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}\sup_{(s,\nu)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})}\operatorname{D}^{f}(\nu\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))\}+2\lambda\sum_{t=H}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\left\|\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\operatorname{Y}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}\kern 5.0pt.

Finally, using that

\displaystyle\sum_{t=H}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\leq\frac{\gamma^{H}}{1-\gamma}\kern 5.0pt,\quad\sum_{t=H}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}(t-1)\leq 2\gamma^{H}\frac{H}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\kern 5.0pt,\quad\sum_{t=H}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}t\leq 2\gamma^{H}\frac{H+1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\kern 5.0pt,

combined with
Lemma

B.6
completes the proof.
∎

Lemma E.4
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
. For any
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim[\nu(\theta)]^{\otimes B}}\left[\left\|\mathrm{g}^{f}(\theta)-\mathrm{g}_{Z}^{f}(\theta)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B}\kern 5.0pt,

where we have defined

\displaystyle\sigma_{f}^{2}

\displaystyle:=\frac{12}{(1-\gamma)^{4}}\left[\omega_{f}^{3}+\lambda^{2}\gamma^{2}\omega_{f}^{3}\mathrm{d}_{f}^{2}+\lambda^{2}(1-\gamma)^{2}\omega_{f}^{2}\mathrm{y}_{f}^{2}\right]\kern 5.0pt,

and where
\omega_{f}
,
\mathrm{d}_{f}
, and
\mathrm{y}_{f}
are defined in
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and (
7
).

Proof.

Firstly, define for
\xi=(s_{h},a_{h})_{h=0}^{H-1}\in(\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A})^{H}

\displaystyle u_{\xi}(\theta)
