\displaystyle:=\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{h}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{\ell}|s_{\ell})}{\partial\theta}\gamma^{h}\mathsf{r}(s_{h},a_{h})

\displaystyle-\lambda\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{h-1}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a_{\ell}|s_{\ell})}{\partial\theta}\gamma^{h}\operatorname{D}^{f}({\pi_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|s_{h})}\|{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s_{h})})-\lambda\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\mathrm{F}_{\theta}^{f}(s_{h})\kern 5.0pt,

Importantly, for a given
Z\sim[\nu(\theta)]^{\otimes B}
, denoting by
Z=(Z_{0},\dots,Z_{B-1})
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathrm{g}_{Z}^{f}(\theta)=\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=0}^{B-1}u_{Z_{b}}(\theta)\kern 5.0pt,\quad\text{ and }\mathrm{g}^{f}(\theta)=\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{T}\sim\nu(\theta)}\left[u_{\mathfrak{T}}(\theta)\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Using that the variables
(Z_{0},\dots,Z_{B-1})
are independent and identically distributed, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim[\nu(\theta)]^{\otimes B}}\left[\left\|\mathrm{g}^{f}(\theta)-\mathrm{g}_{Z}^{f}(\theta)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim[\nu(\theta)]^{\otimes B}}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=0}^{B-1}u_{Z_{b}}(\theta)-\mathrm{g}^{f}(\theta)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{B}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{T}\sim\nu(\theta)}\left[\left\|u_{\mathfrak{T}}(\theta)-\mathrm{g}^{f}(\theta)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq\frac{1}{B}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{T}\sim\nu(\theta)}\left[\left\|u_{\mathfrak{T}}(\theta)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\kern 5.0pt,

(70)

where in the last inequality, we used that the second moment of a random variable dominates its variance. Next, using Jensen’s inequality combined with the convexity of the square function, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{T}\sim\nu(\theta)}\left[\left\|u_{\mathfrak{T}}(\theta)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 3\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{T}}\left[\left\|\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{h}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}{\partial\theta}\gamma^{h}\mathsf{r}(S_{h},A_{h})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+3\lambda^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{T}\sim\nu(\theta)}\left[\left\|\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{h-1}\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}{\partial\theta}\gamma^{h}\operatorname{D}^{f}({\pi_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|S_{h})}\|{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|S_{h})})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+3\lambda^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{T}\sim\nu(\theta)}\left[\left\|\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\mathrm{F}_{\theta}^{f}(S_{h})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\kern 5.0pt,

Applying the triangle inequality and the fact that the reward and the divergence are positive yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{T}\sim\nu(\theta)}\left[\left\|u_{\mathfrak{T}}(\theta)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 3\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{T}\sim\nu(\theta)}\left[\left(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{h}\gamma^{h}\left\|\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}{\partial\theta}\right\|_{2}\mathsf{r}(S_{h},A_{h})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+3\lambda^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{T}\sim\nu(\theta)}\left[\left(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{h-1}\gamma^{h}\left\|\frac{\partial\log\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}{\partial\theta}\right\|_{2}\operatorname{D}^{f}({\pi_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|S_{h})}\|{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|S_{h})})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+3\lambda^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{T}\sim\nu(\theta)}\left[\left(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\left\|\mathrm{F}_{\theta}^{f}(S_{h})\right\|_{2}\right)^{2}\right]\kern 5.0pt,

Combining
Lemma

E.1
and the fact that the reward is bounded between
0
and
1
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{T}}\left[\left\|u_{\mathfrak{T}}(\theta)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq 3\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{T}\sim\nu(\theta)}\left[\left(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{h}2\gamma^{h/2}\cdot\gamma^{h/2}\frac{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(S_{\ell})\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+3\lambda^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{T}\sim\nu(\theta)}\left[\left(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{h-1}2\gamma^{h/2}\cdot\gamma^{h/2}\frac{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(S_{\ell})\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})}\sup_{(s,\nu)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})}\operatorname{D}^{f}(\nu\|{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s)})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+3\lambda^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{T}\sim\nu(\theta)}\left[\left(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}2\gamma^{h/2}\cdot\gamma^{h/2}\left\|\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\operatorname{Y}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}\right)^{2}\right]\kern 5.0pt,

Next, applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{T}\sim\nu(\theta)}\left[\left\|u_{\mathfrak{T}}(\theta)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq 3\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{T}\sim\nu(\theta)}\left[\left(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{h}4\gamma^{h}\right)\left(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{h}\gamma^{h}\frac{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(S_{\ell})^{2}\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})^{2}}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})^{2}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+3\lambda^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{T}\sim\nu(\theta)}\left[\left(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{h-1}4\gamma^{h}\right)\left(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{h-1}\gamma^{h}\frac{\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}(S_{\ell})^{2}\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})^{2}}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(A_{\ell}|S_{\ell})^{2}}\sup_{(s,\nu)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})}\operatorname{D}^{f}(\nu\|{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s)})^{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+3\lambda^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{T}\sim\nu(\theta)}\left[\left(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}4\gamma^{h}\right)\left(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\left\|\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\left\|\operatorname{Y}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\right)\right]\kern 5.0pt,

We define the following filtration, for
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{t}=\sigma(S_{0},A_{0},\dots,S_{t})\kern 5.0pt,

Next, applying the tower property of the conditional expectation by conditioning on
\mathcal{G}_{t}
, and using that
\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(\cdot|s)\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})
for any
s\in\mathcal{S}
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{T}\sim\nu(\theta)}\left[\left\|u_{\mathfrak{T}}(\theta)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq 12\left\|\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\max_{(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}}\left\{\frac{\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}\right\}\left(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}(h+1)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+12\lambda^{2}\left\|\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\max_{(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}}\left\{\frac{\operatorname{w}_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}{\pi_{\theta}^{f}(a|s)}\right\}\sup_{(s,\nu)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})}\left\{\operatorname{D}^{f}(\nu\|{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s)})^{2}\right\}\left(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}h\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+12\lambda^{2}\left\|\operatorname{W}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\left\|\operatorname{Y}_{\theta}^{f}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\left(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\right)^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

Next using

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\kern 5.0pt,\quad\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}t\leq\frac{\gamma}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\kern 5.0pt,\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}(t+1)\leq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\kern 5.0pt,

and plugging in the obtained bound in (
70
), combined with
Lemma

B.6
concludes the proof.
∎

E.2
Sample complexity of Stochastic
f
-PG

We now derive convergence rates for
f
-PG
.
First, we define the following quantity, which will be the Polyak-Łojasiewicz constant of our function over the optimization space, where policies are guaranteed not to be too ill-conditioned, i.e., all their entries are larger than the
\tau_{\lambda}
defined in (
61
),

\displaystyle\underline{\mu}_{f}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\lambda(1-\gamma)\rho_{\min}^{2}\zeta_{f}^{2}}{\omega_{f}^{2}}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}\min_{x\in[\tau_{\lambda},\frac{1}{\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}}]}f^{\prime\prime}(x)^{-2}\kern 5.0pt.

(71)

As we will prove in this subsection, this quantity represents a lower bound of the non-uniform Łojasiewicz coefficient along the trajectory. In the following lemma, we give a simpler lower bound of
\underline{\mu}_{f}
provided that
\lambda
is not too large.

Lemma E.5
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
and
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
respectively. Assume in addition that the initial distribution
\rho
satisfies
\textbf{A}_{\rho}
and that
\lambda
satisfies

\displaystyle\lambda\leq\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\min\left(\frac{4}{\left|f^{\prime}(\iota_{f})\right|},\frac{1}{\left|f^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2})\right|},\frac{4}{\left|f^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})\right|}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

In this case, it holds that

\displaystyle\underline{\mu}_{f}=\frac{\lambda(1-\gamma)\rho_{\min}^{2}\zeta_{f}^{2}}{\omega_{f}^{2}}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}(f^{\star})^{\prime\prime}\left(-\frac{16+8\gamma\lambda\mathrm{d}_{f}}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right)^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

Additionally if
\lambda\leq 1/\mathrm{d}_{f}
, then it holds that

\displaystyle\underline{\mu}_{f}\geq\frac{\lambda(1-\gamma)\rho_{\min}^{2}\zeta_{f}^{2}}{\omega_{f}^{2}}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}(f^{\star})^{\prime\prime}\left(-\frac{24}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right)^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

First note that the first condition on
\lambda
implies that
\tau_{\lambda}<\iota_{f}
,
with
\iota_{f}
defined in
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
, and thus

\displaystyle\min_{x\in[\tau_{\lambda},\frac{1}{\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}}]}f^{\prime\prime}(x)^{-2}=f^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\lambda})^{-2}.

Additionally, the second and third conditions on
\lambda
guarantee that the minimum of
\tau_{\lambda}
in (
61
) is attained in the first term, that is

\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda}=[f^{\prime}]^{-1}\left(-\frac{16+8\gamma\lambda\mathrm{d}_{f}}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Finally, we recall that the convex conjugate of
f
defined in (
28
) satisfies, for any
y\in(-\infty,f^{\prime}(\frac{1}{\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}}))
,

\displaystyle(f^{\star})^{\prime\prime}(y)=\frac{1}{f^{\prime\prime}([f^{\prime}]^{-1}(y))}\kern 5.0pt.

Thus, we obtain that
f^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\lambda})^{-2}=(f^{\star})^{\prime\prime}(\frac{-16+8\gamma\lambda\mathrm{d}_{f}}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}})^{2}
which concludes the proof.
∎

In the following, we define the filtration adapted to the iterates of
f
-PG
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{t}:=\sigma\Big(Z_{t}:t\in\{0,\dots,T-1\}\Big)\kern 5.0pt.

The following theorem gives convergence rates of
f
-PG
.

Theorem E.6
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
and
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
respectively. Assume in addition that the initial distribution
\rho
satisfies
\textbf{A}_{\rho}
. Fix
\eta\leq 1/2L_{f}
, a given temperature
\lambda
, and consider the iterates
(\theta_{t})_{t=0}^{\infty}
of the algorithm
f
-PG
. It holds almost surely that

\displaystyle\inf_{t\geq 0}\mu_{f}(\theta_{t})\geq\underline{\mu}_{f}\kern 5.0pt.

(72)

Additionally, for any
t\geq 0
we have that

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-\mathbb{E}\left[v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)\right]\leq(1-\underline{\mu}_{f}\eta/4)^{t}(v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{0}}}^{f}(\rho))+\frac{6\eta\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B\underline{\mu}_{f}}+\frac{6\beta_{f}^{2}}{\underline{\mu}_{f}}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

Recall that for any
t\in[T]
, we have that

\displaystyle\theta_{t}=\mathcal{T}_{\tau_{\lambda}}(\bar{\theta}_{t})\kern 5.0pt.

Hence, by
Lemma

D.2
, for any
t\in[T]
it holds that

\displaystyle\pi_{\theta_{t}}^{f}\geq\tau_{\lambda}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}\kern 5.0pt.

Combining the previous inequality with the expression of the coefficient
\mu_{f}
provided in
Theorem

C.1
proves the first statement of the lemma. Next, using
Theorem

B.12
gives

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t+1}}}^{f}(\rho)\geq v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)+2\eta\langle\nabla v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho),\mathrm{g}_{Z_{t}}^{f}(\theta_{t})\rangle-\frac{\eta^{2}L_{f}}{2}\left\|\mathrm{g}_{Z_{t}}^{f}(\theta_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

Next, taking the conditional expectation with respect to
\mathcal{F}_{t}
and adding and subtracting
\nabla v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)
in the dot product gives

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t+1}}}^{f}(\rho)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]&\geq v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)+2\eta\left\|\nabla v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)\right\|^{2}+\underbrace{2\eta\langle\nabla v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho),\mathrm{g}^{f}(\theta_{t})-\nabla v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)\rangle}_{\mathbf{(K_{1})}}-\frac{\eta^{2}L_{f}}{2}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathrm{g}_{Z_{t}}^{f}(\theta_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]}_{\mathbf{(K_{2})}}\kern 5.0pt.\end{split}

(73)

We now bound each of these terms separately.

Bounding
\textbf{K}_{1}
.

Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, yields

\displaystyle 2\eta\langle\nabla v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho),\mathrm{g}^{f}(\theta_{t})-\nabla v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)\rangle

\displaystyle\geq-2\eta\left\|\nabla v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)\right\|_{2}\left\|\mathrm{g}^{f}(\theta_{t})-\nabla v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=-2\cdot\eta^{1/2}\left\|\nabla v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)\right\|_{2}\cdot\eta^{1/2}\beta_{f}\kern 5.0pt,

where in the last inequality we used
Lemma

E.3
. Next, using Young’s inequality gives

\displaystyle 2\eta\langle\nabla v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho),\mathrm{g}^{f}(\theta_{t})-\nabla v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)\rangle\geq-\eta\left\|\nabla v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)\right\|_{2}^{2}-\eta\beta_{f}^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

(74)

Bounding
\textbf{K}_{2}
.

Using the convexity of the square function with Jensen’s inequality gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathrm{g}_{Z_{t}}^{f}(\theta_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathrm{g}_{Z_{t}}^{f}(\theta_{t})-\mathrm{g}^{f}{Z_{t}}(\theta_{t})+\mathrm{g}^{f}{Z_{t}}(\theta_{t})-\nabla v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)+\nabla v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)\right\|_{2}^{2}\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 3\beta_{f}^{2}+3\left\|\nabla v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{3\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B}\kern 5.0pt,

(75)

where we used
Lemma

E.3
and
Lemma

E.4
.
Plugging in the bounds (
74
) on
\textbf{K}_{1}
and (
75
) on
\textbf{K}_{2}
in (
73
) gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t+1}}}^{f}(\rho)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]\!\geq\!v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)\!+\!\eta\left\|\nabla v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)\right\|^{2}\!\!-\!\Big(\eta+\frac{3\eta^{2}L_{f}}{2}\Big)\beta_{f}^{2}-\frac{3\eta^{2}L_{f}}{2}\left\|\nabla v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)\right\|_{2}^{2}\!-\!\frac{3\eta^{2}L_{f}\sigma_{f}^{2}}{2B}\kern 5.0pt.

Taking the expectation with respect to all the stochasticity, multiplying both sides by
-1
, and adding
v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)
gives

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-\mathbb{E}\left[v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t+1}}}^{f}(\rho)\right]

\displaystyle\leq v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-\mathbb{E}\left[v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)\right]-\eta\Big(1-\frac{3\eta L_{f}}{2}\Big)\left\|\nabla v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt+\Big(\eta+\frac{3\eta^{2}L_{f}}{2}\Big)\beta_{f}^{2}+\frac{3\eta^{2}L_{f}\sigma_{f}^{2}}{2B}\kern 5.0pt.

Next using
Theorem

C.1
combined with (
72
) yields

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-\mathbb{E}\left[v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t+1}}}^{f}(\rho)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\eta\underline{\mu}_{f}\Big(1-\frac{3\eta L_{f}}{2}\Big)\right)\left(v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-\mathbb{E}\left[v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)\right]\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt+2\eta\beta_{f}^{2}+\frac{3\eta^{2}L_{f}\sigma_{f}^{2}}{2B}\kern 5.0pt,

where we used
3\eta L_{f}/2\leq 1
.
Finally, using that
\eta\leq 1/2L_{f}
to bound
1-3\eta L_{f}/2
and unrolling the recursion concludes the proof.
∎
Next, we provide the sample complexity of
f
-PG
for solving the
f
-regularized objective.

Corollary E.7
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
and
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
respectively. Assume in addition that the initial distribution
\rho
satisfies
\textbf{A}_{\rho}
. Fix any
\epsilon>0
and
\lambda>0
. Setting

\displaystyle H\geq\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\log\left(\frac{216\omega_{f}^{2}}{\epsilon\underline{\mu}_{f}(1-\gamma)^{4}}\left[4+4\lambda^{2}\mathrm{d}_{f}^{2}+\lambda^{2}(1-\gamma)^{2}\mathrm{y}_{f}^{2}\right]\right),

(76)

and

\displaystyle\eta\leq\min\left(\frac{1}{2L_{f}},\frac{\epsilon B\underline{\mu}_{f}}{18\sigma_{f}^{2}}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

(77)

and

\displaystyle T\geq\frac{4}{\underline{\mu}_{f}}\max\left(2L_{f},\frac{18\sigma_{f}^{2}}{\epsilon B\underline{\mu}_{f}}\right)\cdot\log\left(\frac{3(v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{0}}}^{f}(\rho))}{\epsilon}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

(78)

guarantees that

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-\mathbb{E}\left[v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)\right]\leq\epsilon\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

As
\eta\leq 1/2L_{f}
, then by using
Theorem

E.6
, it holds that

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-\mathbb{E}\left[v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{T}}}^{f}(\rho)\right]\leq\underbrace{(1-\underline{\mu}_{f}\eta/4)^{T}(v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{0}}}^{f}(\rho))}_{\mathbf{(U)}}+\underbrace{\frac{6\eta\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B\underline{\mu}_{f}}}_{\mathbf{(V)}}+\underbrace{\frac{6\beta_{f}^{2}}{\underline{\mu}_{f}}}_{\mathbf{(W)}}\kern 5.0pt.

Next, we aim to show that under our conditions on
T
,
H
, and
\eta
, each of these terms is smaller than
\epsilon/3
.

Bounding
V
.

We start with the term
V
, which gives a condition on the step-size. In particular, setting

\displaystyle\eta\leq\frac{\epsilon B\underline{\mu}_{f}}{18\sigma_{f}^{2}}\kern 5.0pt,

guarantees that
\textbf{V}\leq\epsilon/3
, which, together with
\eta\leq 1/(2L_{f})
, gives the condition (
77
).

Bounding
U
.

In order to ensure that
U
is smaller then
\epsilon/3
, we need
T
to satisfy

\displaystyle T\geq\frac{4}{\eta\underline{\mu}_{f}}\log\left(\frac{\epsilon}{3\left(v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{0}}}^{f}(\rho)\right)}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

which, combined with the inequality
\log(1+x)\leq x
for
x>-1
, ensures that
U
is smaller than
\epsilon/3
, and the condition (
78
) follows from (
77
).

Bounding
W
.

Using
Lemma

E.3
and Jensen’s inequality, it holds that

\displaystyle\textbf{W}\leq\frac{6}{\underline{\mu}_{f}}\cdot\frac{4\gamma^{2H}(H+1)^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{4}}\omega_{f}^{2}\left[12+12\lambda^{2}\mathrm{d}_{f}^{2}+3\lambda^{2}(1-\gamma)^{2}\mathrm{y}_{f}^{2}\right]\kern 5.0pt,

Next, we remark that for any
a>0
, we have
a\gamma^{2H}(H+1)^{2}\leq\epsilon/3
for

\displaystyle H\geq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\max\left(\frac{4}{1-\gamma},\log\left(\frac{3a}{\epsilon}\right)\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Taking
a=\frac{6}{\underline{\mu}_{f}}\cdot\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{4}}\omega_{f}^{2}\left[12+12\lambda^{2}\mathrm{d}_{f}^{2}+3\lambda^{2}(1-\gamma)^{2}\mathrm{y}_{f}^{2}\right]
gives
\textbf{W}<\epsilon/3
, provided that (
76
) holds.
∎

E.3
Guarantees on the non-regularized problem

A key criterion for evaluating the quality of a reinforcement learning algorithm is its sample efficiency in solving the original, unregularized objective. To this end, we recall a result from
[
9
]
, which establishes a connection between regularized and unregularized value functions, and further characterizes the performance of the optimal
f
-regularized policy when evaluated in the original unregularized MDP.

Lemma E.8

(Proposition 3 and Theorem 2 of
[
9
]
)
.

For any policy
\pi
, and state
s\in\mathcal{S}
it holds that

\displaystyle\left|v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}^{f}(s)-v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi}(s)\right|\leq\frac{\lambda\mathrm{d}_{f}}{1-\gamma}\kern 5.0pt.

Additionally, denote by
\pi_{\star}^{f}
the optimal regularized policy. For any state
s\in\mathcal{S}
, It holds that

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(s)-v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\pi_{\star}^{f}}\leq\frac{\lambda\mathrm{d}_{f}}{1-\gamma}\kern 5.0pt.

The following theorem gives the convergence rate for the non-regularised problem.

Theorem E.9
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
and
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
respectively. Assume in addition that the initial distribution
\rho
satisfies
\textbf{A}_{\rho}
. Fix
\eta\leq 1/2L_{f}
, a given temperature
\lambda
, and consider the iterates
(\theta)_{t=0}^{\infty}
of the algorithm
f
-PG
. For any
t\geq 0
we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}(\rho)-v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}(\rho)\right]\leq(1-\underline{\mu}_{f}\eta/4)^{t}(v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{0}}}^{f}(\rho))+\frac{6\eta\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B\underline{\mu}_{f}}+\frac{6\beta_{f}^{2}}{\underline{\mu}_{f}}+\frac{2\lambda\mathrm{d}_{f}}{1-\gamma}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

The proof holds from
Theorem

E.6
and
Lemma

E.8
.
∎

Finally, we give the sample complexity of
f
-PG
to solve the unregularised problem.

Corollary E.10
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
f
and
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
and
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
respectively. Assume in addition that the initial distribution
\rho
satisfies
\textbf{A}_{\rho}
. Consider any constant
\mathrm{c}_{f}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{c}_{f}\leq\min\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{d}_{f}},\frac{1}{\mathrm{y}_{f}},1\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Fix any
(1-\gamma)^{-1}>\epsilon>0
such that

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\epsilon<\frac{16}{(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}}\min\left(\frac{4}{\left|f^{\prime}(\iota_{f})\right|},\frac{1}{\left|f^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2})\right|},\frac{4}{\left|f^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})\right|}\right)\!\kern 5.0pt,\kern 5.0pt\!\text{ and set }\lambda=\frac{(1-\gamma)\epsilon}{4}\mathrm{c}_{f}\kern 5.0pt.

(79)

Define

\displaystyle d(\epsilon)=(f^{\star})^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{-96}{\epsilon\mathrm{c}_{f}(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}}\right)^{2}\kern 5.0pt,\quad\ell(\epsilon)=\log\left(\frac{6(v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{0}}}^{f}(\rho))}{\epsilon}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(80)

Additionally, define the three following constants which depend only on
f
as

\displaystyle C_{f}^{(1)}=\frac{16\omega_{f}^{2}}{\mathrm{c}_{f}\zeta_{f}^{2}}\kern 5.0pt,\quad C_{f}^{(2)}=48\omega_{f}\left(\gamma\omega_{f}+(1-\gamma)\kappa_{f}\right)\kern 5.0pt,\quad C_{f}^{(3)}=\frac{3456\omega_{f}^{5}}{\zeta_{f}^{2}\mathrm{c}_{f}}\kern 5.0pt.

(81)

Setting

\displaystyle H\geq\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\log\left(\frac{297\omega_{f}^{2}C_{1}^{f}}{\epsilon d(\epsilon)(1-\gamma)^{6}\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

and

\displaystyle\eta\leq\min\left(\frac{(1-\gamma)^{3}}{C_{f}^{(2)}},\frac{\epsilon^{2}d(\epsilon)(1-\gamma)^{6}B\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}{C_{f}^{(3)}}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

(82)

and

\displaystyle T\geq\frac{16C_{f}^{(1)}\ell(\epsilon)}{\epsilon d(\epsilon)(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}\max\left(\frac{C_{f}^{(2)}}{(1-\gamma)^{3}},\frac{C_{f}^{(3)}}{\epsilon^{2}d(\epsilon)(1-\gamma)^{6}B\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

(83)

guarantees that

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}(\rho)-\mathbb{E}\left[v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}(\rho)\right]\leq\epsilon\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

First, note that the conditions (
79
) on
\epsilon
and
\lambda
guarantee that

\displaystyle\lambda\leq\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\min\left(\frac{4}{\left|f^{\prime}(\iota_{f})\right|},\frac{1}{\left|f^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2})\right|},\frac{4}{\left|f^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})\right|}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

Thus, using
Lemma

E.5
, and the fact that
\epsilon<(1-\gamma)^{-1}
, we have that

\displaystyle\underline{\mu}_{f}\geq\frac{\lambda(1-\gamma)\rho_{\min}^{2}\zeta_{f}^{2}}{\omega_{f}^{2}}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}(f^{\star})^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{-24}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right)^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

Plugging in the expression of
\lambda
from (
79
) yields the following simplified expression

\displaystyle\underline{\mu}_{f}\geq\frac{\epsilon\mathrm{c}_{f}(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}^{2}\zeta_{f}^{2}}{4\omega_{f}^{2}}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}(f^{\star})^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{-96}{\epsilon\mathrm{c}_{f}(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}}\right)^{2}=\frac{4\epsilon(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}{C_{f}^{(1)}}d(\epsilon)\kern 5.0pt,

(84)

where
d(\epsilon)
and
C_{f}^{(1)}
are defined respectively in (
80
) and (
81
).
Additionally, combining
Lemma

E.3
,
Lemma

E.4
and
Theorem

B.12
and the expression of
\lambda
yields

\displaystyle\sigma_{f}^{2}\leq\frac{24}{(1-\gamma)^{4}}\omega_{f}^{3}\kern 5.0pt,\quad\beta_{f}\leq\frac{6\gamma^{H}H}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\omega_{f}\kern 5.0pt,\quad L_{f}=\frac{13\omega_{f}\left(\omega_{f}+(1-\gamma)\kappa_{f}\right)}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}\kern 5.0pt,

(85)

where we used that under
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
, we have
\omega_{f}\geq 1
.
Using
Theorem

E.9
and the fact that
\lambda\leq(1-\gamma)\epsilon/4\mathrm{d}_{f}
, we have that

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}(\rho)-\mathbb{E}\left[v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}(\rho)\right]\leq\underbrace{(1-\underline{\mu}_{f}\eta/4)^{t}(v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{0}}}^{f}(\rho))}_{\mathbf{(U^{\prime})}}+\underbrace{\frac{6\eta\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B\underline{\mu}_{f}}}_{\mathbf{(V^{\prime})}}+\underbrace{\frac{6\beta_{f}^{2}}{\underline{\mu}_{f}}}_{\mathbf{(W^{\prime})}}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\kern 5.0pt.

Next, we aim to show that under our conditions on
T
,
H
, and
\eta
, each of these terms is smaller than
\epsilon/6
.

Bounding
V’
.

We start with the term
V’
which gives a condition on the step-size. Using (
84
) and (
85
), it holds that

\displaystyle\!\textbf{V'}\!\leq\!\frac{144\eta\omega_{f}^{3}}{(1-\gamma)^{4}B\underline{\mu}_{f}}\!\leq\!\frac{576\eta\omega_{f}^{5}}{(1-\gamma)^{6}B}\frac{1}{\epsilon\mathrm{c}_{f}\rho_{\min}^{2}\zeta_{f}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}(f^{\star})^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{-96}{\epsilon\mathrm{c}_{f}(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}}\right)^{2}}=\frac{C_{f}^{(3)}\eta}{6\epsilon d(\epsilon)(1-\gamma)^{6}B\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}},

where
C_{f}^{(3)}
is defined in (
81
).
In particular, setting

\displaystyle\eta\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}d(\epsilon)(1-\gamma)^{6}B\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}{C_{f}^{(3)}}\kern 5.0pt,

guarantees that
\textbf{V'}^{\prime}\leq\epsilon/6
, which together with the condition
\eta\leq 1/(2L_{f})
, gives the condition (
82
).

Bounding
U’
.

In order to ensure that
U’
is smaller then
\epsilon/6
, we need
T
to satisfy

\displaystyle T\geq\frac{1}{\log(1-\eta\underline{\mu}_{f}/4)}\log\left(\frac{\epsilon}{6\left(v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{0}}}^{f}(\rho)\right)}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

which combined with the inequality
\log(1+x)\leq x
for
x>-1
, ensures that
\textbf{V'}^{\prime}<\epsilon/6
and the condition (
83
) follows from (
82
).

Bounding
W’
.

Using (
85
), it holds that

\displaystyle\textbf{W'}\leq\frac{6}{\underline{\mu}_{f}}\cdot\frac{36\gamma^{2H}(H+1)^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{4}}\omega_{f}^{2}\kern 5.0pt.\kern 5.0pt,

Next, using that for any
a>0
, we have
a\gamma^{2H}(H+1)^{2}\leq\epsilon/6
for

\displaystyle H\geq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\max\left(\frac{4}{1-\gamma},\log\left(\frac{6a}{\epsilon}\right)\right)\kern 5.0pt,

shows that under our condition on
H
, we have
\textbf{W'}<\epsilon/6
.
∎

Appendix F
Application to common
f
-divergences

In this section, we apply the results of the preceding section to two commonly used
f
-divergences, which are Kullback-Leibler and
\alpha
-Tsallis.

F.1
Kullback-Leibler

Lemma F.1
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
. The function
f
defined by
f(u)=u\log(u)
satisfies
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
, with

\displaystyle\omega_{f}=1\kern 5.0pt,\quad\kappa_{f}=1\kern 5.0pt,\quad\iota_{f}=1\kern 5.0pt.

Additionally, under the condition that

\displaystyle\lambda\leq\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}(\log(2/\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})+1)}\kern 5.0pt,

(86)

we have

\displaystyle\zeta_{f}=1\kern 5.0pt,\quad\mathrm{d}_{f}\leq|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|\kern 5.0pt,\quad\mathrm{y}_{f}\leq 1+2|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|\kern 5.0pt,\quad L_{f}=\frac{8}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}+4\lambda\frac{3+4|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\beta_{f}=\frac{2\gamma^{H}(H+1)}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left[2+\lambda+4\lambda|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|\right]\kern 5.0pt,\quad\sigma_{f}^{2}\leq\frac{12}{(1-\gamma)^{4}}\left[1+\lambda^{2}\left(2+5|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|^{2}\right)\right]\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\underline{\mu}_{f}\geq\lambda(1-\gamma)\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}\exp\!\left(-\frac{32+16\gamma\lambda|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right)/9\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

Firstly, note that we have

\displaystyle f(u)=u\log(u)\kern 5.0pt,\quad f^{\prime}(u)=\log(u)+1\kern 5.0pt,\quad f^{\prime\prime}(u)=u^{-1}\kern 5.0pt,\quad f^{\prime\prime\prime}(u)=-u^{-2}\kern 5.0pt.

Satisfying
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
.

Observe that
(i)
and
(ii)
are immediately valid from the expression above of the derivatives of
f
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle 1/uf^{\prime\prime}(u)=1\kern 5.0pt,\quad\frac{|f^{\prime\prime\prime}(u)|}{f^{\prime\prime}(u)^{2}}=1

showing that
(iii)
of
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
, is satisfied with
\omega_{f}=\kappa_{f}=1
. Finally, as
f^{\prime\prime}
is a strictly decreasing function on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
then
(iv)
is valid with
\iota_{f}=1
.

Bounding the constants.

Next, we bound sequentially each of the constants that appear in the statement of the lemma. For any
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
\nu\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})
, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}{f^{\prime\prime}(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)})}=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\nu(a)=1\kern 5.0pt,

Thus using (
9
), we have that
\zeta_{f}=1
.
It holds that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{f}

\displaystyle=\max_{(s,\nu)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)f(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)})

\displaystyle=\max_{(s,\nu)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\nu(a)\log(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)})

\displaystyle\leq\max_{(s,\nu)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\nu(a)\log(\nu(a))-\nu(a)\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})\kern 5.0pt,

which gives
\mathrm{d}_{f}\leq-\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
.
Next, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{y}_{f}

\displaystyle=\max_{(s,\nu)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}{f^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)}\left|f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\max_{(s,\nu)\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\nu(a)\left|\log\left(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)+1\right|

\displaystyle=1-\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})+\max_{\nu\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})}-\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\nu(a)\log(\nu(a))

\displaystyle\leq 1+2|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|\kern 5.0pt,

where in the last inequality, we used that the entropy of a distribution on
\mathcal{A}
is bounded by
\log(|\mathcal{A}|)
and the fact that
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}\leq 1/|\mathcal{A}|
. Next, using
Theorem

B.12
and the bounds above, we have

\displaystyle L_{f}

\displaystyle=\frac{8\omega_{f}\left(\gamma\omega_{f}+(1-\gamma)\kappa_{f}\right)}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}+4\lambda\frac{2\gamma^{2}\omega_{f}^{2}\mathrm{d}_{f}+2\gamma(1-\gamma)\omega_{f}\left[\kappa_{f}\mathrm{d}_{f}+\mathrm{y}_{f}\right]+(1-\gamma)^{2}\left[\omega_{f}+2\kappa_{f}\mathrm{y}_{f}\right]}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}+4\lambda\frac{3+4|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}\kern 5.0pt.

Using
Lemma

E.3
gives

\displaystyle\beta_{f}=\frac{2\gamma^{H}(H+1)}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\omega_{f}\left[2+2\lambda\mathrm{d}_{f}+\lambda(1-\gamma)\mathrm{y}_{f}\right]\leq\frac{2\gamma^{H}(H+1)}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left[2+\lambda+4\lambda|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Using
Lemma

E.4
gives

\displaystyle\sigma_{f}^{2}=\frac{12}{(1-\gamma)^{4}}\left[\omega_{f}^{3}+\lambda^{2}\gamma^{2}\omega_{f}^{3}\mathrm{d}_{f}^{2}+\lambda^{2}(1-\gamma)^{2}\omega_{f}^{2}\mathrm{y}_{f}^{2}\right]\leq\frac{12}{(1-\gamma)^{4}}\left[1+\lambda^{2}(2+5|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|^{2}))\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Next, note that (
86
), guarantees that we have

\displaystyle\lambda\leq\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\min\left(\frac{4}{\left|f^{\prime}(\iota_{f})\right|},\frac{1}{\left|f^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2})\right|},\frac{4}{\left|f^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})\right|}\right)

Thus, using
Lemma

E.5
, we have

\displaystyle\underline{\mu}_{f}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda(1-\gamma)\rho_{\min}^{2}\zeta_{f}^{2}}{\omega_{f}^{2}}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}(f^{\star})^{\prime\prime}\left(-\frac{16+8\gamma\lambda\mathrm{d}_{f}}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right)^{2}\geq\lambda(1-\gamma)\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}\exp\left(-\frac{32+16\gamma\lambda|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right)/9\kern 5.0pt,

where in the last equality, we used that the convex conjugate of
f(u)=u\log(u)
is
f^{\star}(y)=\exp(y-1)
and that
\exp(-2)\geq 1/9
.
∎
In the next two corollaries, we apply
Corollary

E.7
and
Corollary

E.10
to get more explicitly the sample complexity of
f
-PG
with entropy regularization.

Corollary F.2
.

Assume that, for some
1/4\geq\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}>0
,
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
. Assume in addition that the initial distribution
\rho
satisfies
\textbf{A}_{\rho}
. Fix any
(1-\gamma)^{-1}\geq\epsilon>0
,
\lambda>0
and
B
such that

\displaystyle\lambda\leq\min\left(\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}(\log(2/\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})+1)},1\right)\kern 5.0pt,\quad B\leq\frac{216|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}\rho_{\min}^{2}}\exp\!\left(\frac{48|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Setting

\displaystyle H\geq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\log\left(\frac{29160\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})^{2}}{\epsilon\lambda(1-\gamma)^{5}\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}\right)+\frac{52|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}},

(87)

and

\displaystyle\eta\leq\frac{\epsilon B\lambda(1-\gamma)^{5}\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}{15552|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|^{2}}\exp\!\left(-\frac{48|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

and

\displaystyle T\geq\frac{559872|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|^{2}}{\lambda^{2}\epsilon B(1-\gamma)^{6}\rho_{\min}^{4}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{4}}\exp\!\left(\frac{96|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

guarantees that

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-\mathbb{E}\left[v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}^{f}(\rho)\right]\leq\epsilon\kern 5.0pt,

where
f
is the Kullback-Leibler divergence generator.
Thus, the sample complexity of
f
-PG
to learn an
\epsilon
-solution of the entropy regularized problem is

\displaystyle TBH\approx\frac{1}{\lambda^{3}\epsilon B}\frac{|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|^{3}}{(1-\gamma)^{9}\rho_{\min}^{5}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{4}}\exp\!\left(\frac{|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right)

Proof.

To prove this corollary, we show that the assumptions of
Corollary

E.7
hold.
First,
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
holds as a consequence of
Lemma

F.1
.
Then, we show that the condition (
76
) in
Corollary

E.7
holds, that is
H\leq\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\log\left(\tfrac{216\omega_{f}^{2}}{\epsilon\underline{\mu}_{f}(1-\gamma)^{4}}\left[4+4\lambda^{2}\mathrm{d}_{f}^{2}+\lambda^{2}(1-\gamma)^{2}\mathrm{y}_{f}^{2}\right]\right)
.
To this end, we remark that

\displaystyle\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\log\left(\frac{216\omega_{f}^{2}}{\epsilon\underline{\mu}_{f}(1-\gamma)^{4}}\left[4+4\lambda^{2}\mathrm{d}_{f}^{2}+\lambda^{2}(1-\gamma)^{2}\mathrm{y}_{f}^{2}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\log\left(\frac{216}{\epsilon\underline{\mu}_{f}(1-\gamma)^{4}}\left[4+\lambda^{2}(5+6\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})^{2})\right]\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\log\left(\frac{1944\left[4+\lambda^{2}(5+6\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})^{2})\right]}{\epsilon\lambda(1-\gamma)^{5}\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}\right)+\frac{32+16\gamma\lambda|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\log\left(\frac{29160\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})^{2}}{\epsilon\lambda(1-\gamma)^{5}\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}\right)+\frac{52|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}}\leq H\kern 5.0pt,

where we used the lower bound on
\underline{\mu}_{f}
provided in
Lemma

F.1
in the second inequality, as well as
\lambda<1
and
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}\leq 1/4
in the last two inequalities.
Furthermore,
Lemma

F.1
with
\lambda\leq 1
gives

\displaystyle L_{f}\leq\frac{36|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}\kern 5.0pt,

(88)

\displaystyle\sigma_{f}^{2}\leq\frac{96|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{4}}\kern 5.0pt,

(89)

\displaystyle\underline{\mu}_{f}\geq\frac{\lambda(1-\gamma)\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}{9}\exp\!\left(-\frac{48|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(90)

Using these three bounds on smoothness, variance and Polyak-Łojasiewicz coefficients, we obtain

\displaystyle\min\left(\frac{1}{2L_{f}},\frac{\epsilon B\underline{\mu}_{f}}{18\sigma_{f}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\min\left(\frac{(1-\gamma)^{3}}{72|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|},\frac{\epsilon B\lambda(1-\gamma)^{5}\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}\exp\!\left(-\frac{48|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right)}{15552|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon B\lambda(1-\gamma)^{5}\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}{15552|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|^{2}}\exp\!\left(-\frac{48|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

(91)

where in the last identity, we used the fact that
\epsilon<(1-\gamma)^{-1}
and that

\displaystyle B\leq\frac{216|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}\rho_{\min}^{2}}\exp\!\left(\frac{48|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

This shows that our condition on
\eta
guarantees that the one set in
Corollary

E.7
is satisfied. Finally using (
91
) and (
90
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{4}{\underline{\mu}_{f}}\max\left(2L_{f},\frac{18\sigma_{f}^{2}}{\epsilon B\underline{\mu}_{f}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{\underline{\mu}_{f}}\frac{15552|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|^{2}}{\epsilon B\lambda(1-\gamma)^{5}\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}\exp\!\left(\frac{48|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{559872|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|^{2}}{\lambda^{2}\epsilon B(1-\gamma)^{6}\rho_{\min}^{4}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{4}}\exp\!\left(\frac{96|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

which concludes the proof.
∎

Corollary F.3
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}\in(0,1/4]
,
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
. Assume in addition that the initial distribution
\rho
satisfies
\textbf{A}_{\rho}
and fix
f
to be the Kullback-Leibler divergence generator, i.e.
f(u)=u\log(u)
. Fix any
\epsilon\in(0,(1-\gamma)^{-1}]
, such that

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\epsilon<\frac{16}{(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}(\log(2/\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})+1)}\kern 5.0pt,\kern 5.0pt\!\text{ and set }\lambda=\frac{(1-\gamma)\epsilon}{12\log(|\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}|)}\kern 5.0pt.

(92)

Additionally set any
B
such that

\displaystyle B\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}\exp\left(\frac{576|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(93)

Setting

\displaystyle H\geq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\log\left(\frac{|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon(1-\gamma)^{6}\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}\right)+\frac{586|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon(1-\gamma)^{4}\rho_{\min}}\kern 5.0pt,

and

\displaystyle\eta\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}(1-\gamma)^{6}B\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}{93312|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}\exp\left(\frac{-576|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

(94)

and

\displaystyle T\geq\frac{644972544|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|^{2}}{\epsilon^{3}(1-\gamma)^{8}\rho_{\min}^{4}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{4}B}\exp\left(\frac{1152|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}}\right)\log\left(\frac{6(v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{0}}}^{f}(\rho))}{\epsilon}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

(95)

guarantees that

\displaystyle v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}(\rho)-\mathbb{E}\left[v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{t}}}(\rho)\right]\leq\epsilon\kern 5.0pt.

Thus, the sample complexity of
f
-PG
, where
f
is the Kullback-Leibler divergence generator, to learn an
\epsilon
-solution of the non-regularized problem is

\displaystyle TBH\approx\frac{|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|^{3}}{\epsilon^{4}(1-\gamma)^{12}\rho_{\min}^{5}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{4}}\exp\left(\frac{|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}}\right)

Proof.

This result follows from
Corollary

E.10
, whose assumptions we check now.
First, note that (
92
) implies that

\displaystyle\epsilon<\frac{16}{(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}}\min\left(\frac{4}{\left|f^{\prime}(\iota_{f})\right|},\frac{1}{\left|f^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2})\right|},\frac{4}{\left|f^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})\right|}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Additionally, we can rewrite the constants from
Corollary

E.10
using
Lemma

F.1
, which gives

\displaystyle C_{f}^{(1)}\leq 48|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|,\kern 5.0ptC_{f}^{(2)}\leq 48,\kern 5.0ptC_{f}^{(3)}=10368|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|,\kern 5.0pt\!d(\epsilon)\geq\exp\left(\frac{-576|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}}\right)/9,

(96)

where
C_{f}^{(1)},C_{f}^{(2)},C_{f}^{(3)}
, and
d(\epsilon)
are defined in (
81
) and (
80
). Next, the condition on
H
in
Corollary

E.10
holds since

\displaystyle\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\log\left(\frac{297\omega_{f}^{2}C_{1}^{f}}{\epsilon d(\epsilon)(1-\gamma)^{6}\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\log\left(\frac{128304|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon(1-\gamma)^{6}\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}\right)+\frac{576|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon(1-\gamma)^{4}\rho_{\min}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\log\left(\frac{|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon(1-\gamma)^{6}\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}\right)+\frac{586|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon(1-\gamma)^{4}\rho_{\min}}\leq H\kern 5.0pt,

where in the second to last inequality, we used that
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}<1/4
and that
\log(128304)\leq 6
.
Using (
96
), we have

\displaystyle\min\left(\frac{(1-\gamma)^{3}}{C_{f}^{(2)}},\frac{\epsilon^{2}d(\epsilon)(1-\gamma)^{6}B\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}{C_{f}^{(3)}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\geq\min\left(\frac{(1-\gamma)^{3}}{48},\frac{\epsilon^{2}(1-\gamma)^{6}B\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}{93312|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}\exp\left(\frac{-576|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\geq\frac{\epsilon^{2}(1-\gamma)^{6}B\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}{93312|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}\exp\left(\frac{-576|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

(97)

where in the last inequality, we used the condition on
B
introduced in (
93
). Hence our condition on the step-size ensures that the one assumed in
Corollary

E.10
is satisfied. Next, using (
96
) and (
97
) yields

\displaystyle\frac{16C_{f}^{(1)}\ell(\epsilon)}{\epsilon d(\epsilon)(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}\max\left(\frac{C_{f}^{(2)}}{(1-\gamma)^{3}},\frac{C_{f}^{(3)}}{\epsilon^{2}d(\epsilon)(1-\gamma)^{6}B\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{16C_{f}^{(1)}\ell(\epsilon)}{\epsilon d(\epsilon)(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}\cdot\frac{93312|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon^{2}(1-\gamma)^{6}B\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}\exp\left(\frac{576|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{6912|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|\ell(\epsilon)}{\epsilon(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}\cdot\frac{93312|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon^{2}(1-\gamma)^{6}B\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}\exp\left(\frac{1152|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{644972544|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|^{2}}{\epsilon^{3}(1-\gamma)^{8}\rho_{\min}^{4}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{4}B}\exp\left(\frac{1152|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}}\right)\log\left(\frac{6(v_{\hskip 0.57367pt\star}^{f}(\rho)-v_{\hskip 0.57367pt{\theta_{0}}}^{f}(\rho))}{\epsilon}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

which proves that under our condition on
T
the one assumed by
Corollary

E.10
is satisfied.
∎

F.2
\alpha
-Tsallis

Lemma F.4
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}\in(0,1/4]
,
\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}
satisfy
\textbf{P}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
. For any
\alpha\in(0;1)
, the function
f_{\alpha}
defined by

\displaystyle f_{\alpha}(u)=\frac{u^{\alpha}-\alpha u+\alpha-1}{\alpha(\alpha-1)}\kern 5.0pt,

satisfies
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
, with

\displaystyle\omega_{f}=\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{\alpha-1}\kern 5.0pt,\quad\kappa_{f}=2\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{\alpha-1}\kern 5.0pt,\quad\iota_{f}=1\kern 5.0pt.

Additionally, under the condition that

\displaystyle\lambda\leq\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\cdot\frac{1-\alpha}{(\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}/2)^{\alpha-1}-1}\kern 5.0pt,

(98)

we have

\displaystyle\zeta_{f}=1\kern 5.0pt,\kern 5.0pt\mathrm{d}_{f}\leq\frac{4|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\alpha^{2}}\kern 5.0pt,\kern 5.0pt\mathrm{y}_{f}\leq 4|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|\kern 5.0pt,\kern 5.0ptL_{f}=\frac{16\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{\alpha-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}+180\lambda\frac{\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2\alpha-2}|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\alpha^{2}(1-\gamma)^{3}}\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\beta_{f}=\frac{4\gamma^{H}(H+1)}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left[1+6\lambda\frac{|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\alpha^{2}}\right]\kern 5.0pt,\quad\sigma_{f}^{2}\leq\frac{12\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{3\alpha-3}}{(1-\gamma)^{4}}\left[1+\frac{16\lambda^{2}}{\alpha^{4}}\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}|^{2})\right]\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\underline{\mu}_{f}\geq\lambda(1-\gamma)\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2-2\alpha}\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}\exp_{\alpha}\left(-\frac{16+32\gamma\lambda|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|/\alpha^{2}}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right)^{4-2\alpha}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

Fix any
\alpha\in(0,1)
and set
f=f_{\alpha}
. Firstly, note that we have

\displaystyle f(u)=\frac{u^{\alpha}-\alpha u+\alpha-1}{\alpha(\alpha-1)}\kern 5.0pt,\quad f^{\prime}(u)=\frac{u^{\alpha-1}-1}{\alpha-1}\kern 5.0pt,\quad f^{\prime\prime}(u)=u^{\alpha-2}\kern 5.0pt,\quad f^{\prime\prime\prime}(u)=(\alpha-2)u^{\alpha-3}\kern 5.0pt.

Satisfying
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
.

Observe that
(i)
and
(ii)
are immediately valid from the expression above of the derivatives of
f
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle 1/(uf_{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}(u))=u^{1-\alpha}\kern 5.0pt,\quad\frac{|f^{\prime\prime\prime}(u)|}{f^{\prime\prime}(u)^{2}}=|\alpha-2|u^{1-\alpha}\kern 5.0pt,

showing that
(iii)
of
\textbf{A}_{f}(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})
, is satisfied with
\omega_{f}=\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{\alpha-1}
, and
\kappa_{f}=2\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{\alpha-1}
. Finally,as
f^{\prime\prime}
is a strictly decreasing function on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
then
(iv)
is valid with
\iota_{f}=1
. Next, we bound sequentially each of the constants that appear in the statement of the lemma.

Bounding
\zeta_{f}
.

For any
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
\nu\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})
, using Jensen’s inequality we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}{f^{\prime\prime}(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)})}=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\left(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)^{2-\alpha}\geq\left(\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)^{2-\alpha}=1\kern 5.0pt,

Thus using (
9
), we have that
\zeta_{f}=1
.

Bounding
\mathrm{d}_{f}
.

For any state
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
\nu\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})
, it holds that

\displaystyle\operatorname{D}^{f}(\nu\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))=\frac{1}{\alpha(\alpha-1)}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\left[\left(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)^{\alpha}-\alpha\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}-(1-\alpha)\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Next, for
y\in]0;\frac{1}{\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}}]
, define the function
p_{y}\colon[\alpha;1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
which satisfies

\displaystyle p_{y}(\beta)=y^{\beta}-\beta y-(1-\beta)\kern 5.0pt,\quad p_{y}^{\prime}(\beta)=\log(y)y^{\beta}-y+1\kern 5.0pt.

It holds that

\displaystyle\frac{y^{\alpha}-\alpha y-(1-\alpha)}{\alpha-1}=\frac{p_{y}(\alpha)-p_{y}(1)}{\alpha-1}\leq\sup_{\beta\in[\alpha,1]}|p_{y}^{\prime}(\beta)|

\displaystyle\leq y+1+y|\log(y)|\mathsf{1}_{y\geq 1}+|\log(y)|y^{\alpha}\mathsf{1}_{y\leq 1}\kern 5.0pt.

Applying the previous inequality with
y=\nu(a)/\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)
yields

\displaystyle\operatorname{D}^{f}(\nu\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\bigg[1+\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}+\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}|\log(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)})|\mathsf{1}_{\nu(a)/\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\geq 1}

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt+|\log(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)})|\left(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)^{\alpha}\mathsf{1}_{\nu(a)/\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\leq 1}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\nu(a)\log(\nu(a))\mathsf{1}_{\nu(a)/\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\geq 1}-\frac{1}{\alpha}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\nu(a)\log(\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s))\mathsf{1}_{\nu(a)/\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\geq 1}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\alpha}\max_{a\in\mathcal{A}}|\log(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)})|\left(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)^{\alpha}\mathsf{1}_{\nu(a)/\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\leq 1}+\frac{2}{\alpha}\kern 5.0pt,

where in the last equality, we used that if
x\geq 1
, then
\log(x)\geq 0
. Next using that
\log(\nu(a))\leq 0
yields

\displaystyle\operatorname{D}^{f}(\nu\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{\alpha}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\nu(a)\log(\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s))+\frac{1}{\alpha}\max_{x\in[0;1]}|\log(x)|x^{\alpha}+\frac{2}{\alpha}\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|+\frac{1}{\alpha}\max_{x\in[0;1]}|\log(x)|x^{\alpha}+\frac{2}{\alpha}\kern 5.0pt,

where in the last inequality, we used that the entropy of a probability measure on
\mathcal{A}
is bounded by
|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|
. Next using that
\max_{x\in[0;1]}|\log(x)|x^{\alpha}\leq e^{-1}/\alpha
combined with
\max(1,\log(|\mathcal{A}|))\leq|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|
gives

\displaystyle\operatorname{D}^{f}(\nu\|\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(\cdot|s))\leq\frac{3|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\alpha}+\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}\leq\frac{4|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\alpha^{2}}\kern 5.0pt.

Thus, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{f}\leq\frac{4|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\alpha^{2}}\kern 5.0pt.

Bounding
\mathrm{y}_{f}
.

For any policy
\nu\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})
and
s\in\mathcal{S}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}{f^{\prime\prime}(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)})}\left|f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)\right|=\left|\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\right|\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\nu(a)^{2-\alpha}}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)^{1-\alpha}}\left|\left(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)^{\alpha-1}-1\right|\kern 5.0pt.

Next, define the function
g_{y}
for
y\in]0;\frac{1}{\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}}]
which satisfies

\displaystyle g_{y}(\beta)=y^{\beta-1}\kern 5.0pt,\quad g_{y}^{\prime}(\beta)=\log(y)y^{\beta-1}\kern 5.0pt.

It holds that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{y^{\alpha-1}-1}{1-\alpha}\right|=\left|\frac{g_{y}(\alpha)-g_{y}(1)}{\alpha-1}\right|\leq\sup_{\beta\in[\alpha,1]}\left|g_{y}^{\prime}(\beta)\right|\leq\left|\log(y)\right|\mathsf{1}_{y\leq 1}+\left|\log(y)y^{\alpha-1}\right|\mathsf{1}_{y\geq 1}\kern 5.0pt,

Hence, applying the previous inequality with
y=\nu(a)/\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)
gives

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}{f^{\prime\prime}(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)})}\left|f^{\prime}\left(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 20.0pt\leq\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\nu(a)^{2-\alpha}}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)^{1-\alpha}}\left[\left|\log\left(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)\right|\mathsf{1}_{\nu(a)/\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\leq 1}+\left|\log\left(\frac{\pi(a|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)\right|\left(\frac{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}{\nu(a)}\right)^{1-\alpha}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 20.0pt\leq\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\left(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a)}\right)^{1-\alpha}\nu(a)\left|\log\left(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)\right|\mathsf{1}_{\nu(a)/\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)\leq 1}+\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\nu(a)\left|\log\left(\frac{\pi(a|s)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 20.0pt\leq\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\nu(a)\left|\log\left(\frac{\nu(a)}{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}(a|s)}\right)\right|+2|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|\kern 5.0pt,

where in the last inequality, we used for the first term that for any
u\in\mathbb{R},u\mathsf{1}_{u\leq 1}\leq 1
that the entropy of a probability distribution on
\mathcal{A}
is bounded by
\log(|\mathcal{A}|)
, the fact that
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}\leq 1/|\mathcal{A}|
. Using the same argument again to bound the first term gives

\displaystyle\mathrm{y}_{f}\leq 4|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|\kern 5.0pt.

.

Bounding
L_{f}
.

Next, using
Theorem

B.12
and the bound on the constants previously computed, we have

\displaystyle L_{f}

\displaystyle=\frac{8\omega_{f}\left(\gamma\omega_{f}+(1-\gamma)\kappa_{f}\right)}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}+4\lambda\frac{2\gamma^{2}\omega_{f}^{2}\mathrm{d}_{f}+2\gamma(1-\gamma)\omega_{f}\left[\kappa_{f}\mathrm{d}_{f}+\mathrm{y}_{f}\right]+(1-\gamma)^{2}\left[\omega_{f}+2\kappa_{f}\mathrm{y}_{f}\right]}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{\alpha-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}+180\lambda\frac{\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2\alpha-2}|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\alpha^{2}(1-\gamma)^{3}}\kern 5.0pt,

where in the last inequality, we used that
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}<1/4
.

Bounding

Using

gives

\displaystyle\beta_{f}=\frac{2\gamma^{H}(H+1)}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\omega_{f}\left[2+2\lambda\mathrm{d}_{f}+\lambda(1-\gamma)\mathrm{y}_{f}\right]\leq\frac{4\gamma^{H}(H+1)}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left[1+6\lambda\frac{|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\alpha^{2}}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Bounding
\underline{\mu}_{f}
.

f_{\alpha}^{\prime}
is an increasing function then

(
98
), guarantees that

\displaystyle\lambda\leq\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\min\left(\frac{4}{\left|f^{\prime}(\iota_{f})\right|},\frac{1}{\left|f^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2})\right|},\frac{4}{\left|f^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})\right|}\right)

Thus, using

E.5

\displaystyle\underline{\mu}_{f}=\frac{\lambda(1-\gamma)\rho_{\min}^{2}\zeta_{f}^{2}}{\omega_{f}^{2}}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}(f^{\star})^{\prime\prime}\left(-\frac{16+8\gamma\lambda\mathrm{d}_{f}}{\lambda(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right)^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

(99)

\displaystyle f_{\alpha}^{*}(x)=\frac{\left(1+(\alpha-1)x\right)^{\,\frac{\alpha}{\,\alpha-1\,}}-1}{\alpha}\kern 5.0pt,\quad\text{ for }x\leq\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\kern 5.0pt.

.

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\log\left(\exp_{\alpha}\left(-\frac{16+32\gamma\lambda|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\lambda\alpha^{2}(1-\gamma)^{2}\rho_{\min}}\right)^{4-2\alpha}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

Corollary F.6
.

Assume that, for some
\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}\in(0,1/4]
,

, such that

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\epsilon<\frac{16}{(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}}\cdot\frac{1-\alpha}{(\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}/2)^{\alpha-1}-1}\kern 5.0pt,\kern 5.0pt\!\text{ and set }\lambda=\frac{(1-\gamma)\alpha^{2}\epsilon}{16|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}\kern 5.0pt.

\displaystyle H\geq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\log\left(\frac{19008\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{4\alpha-2}|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon\alpha^{2}(1-\gamma)^{6}\rho_{\min}^{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}}^{2}}\right)+\frac{1540|\log(\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})|}{\epsilon\alpha^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}\rho_{\min}}\kern 5.0pt,

Proof.

\displaystyle\epsilon<\frac{16}{(1-\gamma)^{3}\rho_{\min}}\min\left(\frac{4}{\left|f^{\prime}(\iota_{f})\right|},\frac{1}{\left|f^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2})\right|},\frac{4}{\left|f^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}\underline{\pi_{\operatorname{ref}}})\right|}\right)\kern 5.0pt,\quad\lambda=\frac{(1-\gamma)\epsilon}{4}\mathrm{c}_{f}\kern 5.0pt,
